# French Mathematics Diagnostic Curriculum — Expanded Question-Generation Reference (v11.0)

**Language:** English for the reference; generate student-facing items using `{OUTPUT_LANGUAGE}` (French by default).
**Dates:** regulations assessed on 19 October 2026 (v5); diagnostic layer built on 2 November 2026 (v6); official-anchor expansion and QA pass on 2 November 2026 (v7); audit corrections on 2 November 2026 (v8); consolidated CODEX + GROK audit of v8 applied on 2 November 2026 (v9); consolidated Claude + Codex + Grok audit of v9 applied on 3 November 2026 (v10); consolidated CODEX + Grok audit of v10 applied on 3 November 2026 (v11).
**Basis:** CLAUDE v6 (itself built on CODEX v5 and `French_Math_Curriculum_Reference_EN_CLAUDE_v2.docx`), Codex's validation report on v6 and its deep audit of v7 (mode-aware revision), Grok's review of that audit (2 November 2026, R35), codebook `codebook-v5.1` and the YAML format `alphavalon.eval/v1`; consolidated CODEX + GROK audit of v8 (R38); consolidated audit of v9 (R39); audit of v10 (R40); companion prompt `PROMPT_Diagnostic_Maths_Brevet_Bac_MPI_v9.md`.
**What v6 added:** a diagnostic precision layer (§2C) and, in every row, the wrong answer that each error produces on the seed.
**What v7 adds:** 331 new micro-skills (600 in total), each anchored to an entry of the official programme (Appendix A); an official anchor `ref` on every row; evidence states per observation (§2C.6a); collision and masking fixes (§1.2 item 19); an identifier migration table (Appendix B); reproducible validation scripts and their report (§15).
**What v8 changes (audit of v7):** every errors cell is split by seed (**MCQ seed:** / **Open seed:**) and the CSV gives each error `mode`, `sig_mcq` and `sig_open` (rule R1, §2C.1); 107 MCQ-seed errors added on 94 rows so that every row but one has three distinct MCQ distractors (`allowed_items`; `TC-IN-02` is `OPEN_ONLY`); `BR-AP-01-E3` merged into `E1`; four stems state the required form and `M2-ED-03` is compared as written; 158 rows with a `COPY` or `TOC` anchor are `PROVISIONAL` and listed for a BO check; the validator re-renders every row from the CSV and states its scope (§1.2 items 23–31).
**What v9 changes (CODEX + GROK audit of v8):** the six `1C-FN` derivative rows are quarantined (`HOLD`, anchor `NOT_SUPPORTED`: derivation is not in the 2026 programme, which reverses item 3); the 301 targets of the original CODEX v5 are enumerated in the migration table with proposed, unreviewed candidates (274 proposed, 27 unmapped) and their `EC-*` codes are kept as legacy codes; anchor statuses are `SOURCE_LINKED` (442), `PROVISIONAL` (152) and `NOT_SUPPORTED` (6); the validator reports six release gates and its real coverage; the build is reproducible from the package (§1.2 items 32–38).
**What v10 changes (audit of v9):** the M2-SF-04 reproducibility failure is fixed (floats compared with a tolerance, the series check made exact); the v6 checks run in-process and count as coverage only when linked to the current CSV; every row gets a release eligibility (`Item_Release_Eligibility_v10.csv`, from `Item_Review_Register_v10.csv`); `SOURCE_LINKED` no longer passes the curriculum gate; a seventh gate, `DIAGNOSTIC_VALIDITY_PASS`; the report records the environment (§1.2 items 39–43). Data rows are unchanged from v9.
**What v11 changes (audit of v10):** every PASS needs complete, dated evidence (BO page and passage; teacher date; text-signature review; pilot evidence); paper and digital readiness are separate; exact integers are compared exactly; `Coverage_Gaps_v11.csv` lists every unchecked key and signature; the generated Markdown holds no runtime environment, so the rebuild is identical across Python patch releases; `tests_v11.py` runs the audit's regression scenarios (§1.2 items 44–50). Data rows are unchanged from v9.
**Companion files (v11):** `Math_Diagnostic_Targets_v11.csv`, `Item_Review_Register_v11.csv`, `Item_Release_Eligibility_v11.csv`, `Coverage_Gaps_v11.csv`, `validate_reference_v11.py`, `tests_v11.py`, `build_v11.py`. *(v10:)* `Math_Diagnostic_Targets_v10.csv`, `Item_Review_Register_v10.csv`, `Item_Release_Eligibility_v10.csv`, `validate_reference_v10.py`, `build_v10.py` and the v10 copies of the v9 tables. *(v9:)* `Math_Diagnostic_Targets_v9.csv`, `Math_Official_Referential_Index_v9.csv`, `Math_ID_Migration_to_v9.csv`, `Anchor_Verification_Worklist_v9.csv`, `Legacy_EC_Codes_CODEX_v5.csv`, `validate_reference_v9.py`, `build_v9.py`. *(v8 list:)* `Math_Diagnostic_Targets_v8.csv` (one row per micro-skill; errors as JSON with `mode`, `sig_mcq`, `sig_open`, `origin`, `status`), `Math_Official_Referential_Index_v8.csv`, `Math_ID_Migration_v5_v6_v7_to_v8.csv`, `Anchor_Verification_Worklist_v8.csv`, `validate_reference_v8.py` with `v7lib.py`, `chk_*.py` (including `chk_ADD.py` for the v8 errors), `check_values_v6.py`, and `validation_report_v8.json`.

> **Status note:** This is a detailed **pedagogical design catalogue**, not a replacement for the Bulletin officiel. Items and misconceptions below are **author-created diagnostic seeds** unless quoted explicitly. The original descriptions are preserved as source extracts, and official eligibility must be checked for the specified school year before an assessed item is released. The error mechanisms per micro-skill are hypotheses about likely mistakes, **not claims of empirically documented misconceptions**; document/validate them before labelling them documented in T4. **(v6)** Signatures are computed from the stated error rules (numeric ones re-checked by script); they are not observed student answers. **(v7)** The anchor `ref` names the official capability a row exercises; the row itself remains an authored seed, not an official item.

## 1. Scope, counting and intended usage

- **61 subdomains** (the 60 original ones and `TC-LO`, added in v7), all existing codes retained, across **8 tracks**; **600 micro-skills** (269 from v6, 331 added in v7), one primary competency per row, each anchored to an official programme entry (`ref`, Appendix A).
- **Error mechanisms (v8):** 2 177 active error IDs: the 2 071 of v7, less `BR-AP-01-E3` (merged into `E1`), plus 107 MCQ-seed errors (`E4`–`E6`, marked *(v8)*) added where a row had fewer than three distinct MCQ distractors. By seed: 1 808 on the MCQ seed only, 357 on the open seed only, 12 on both; 737 errors carry transversal tags. **(v7)** 2 071 active error IDs — the 1 078 v6 IDs kept (one padding error retired: `M1-F2-02-E4`) and 993 `E1`–`E3` of the new rows; 726 errors carry transversal tags. **(v6)** 1 079 error IDs — the 807 v5 `E1`–`E3` (meanings unchanged) plus 272 `E4`–`E6` added where E1–E3 could not give three distinct MCQ distractors. Every error now carries its **signature** (wrong answer on the seed) and 0–2 transversal codes; 411 are tagged.
- **Open targets (v6):** the 161 v5 targets marked "To be worked and independently verified" are computed; 39 seeds were changed (masking, collision, guardrail, eligibility) and say why in the row.
- **Transversal error bank:** 97 basic-calculation codes (`CB-*`, 15 families: the 12 v5 families, the 9 codes proposed in codebook v5.1, and the v6 families `CB-DER` derivatives and primitives, `CB-PRB` probability, `CB-LIM` limits and equivalents) and 13 reasoning/proof codes (`RP-*`), a crosswalk generated from the rows, a student error-tracking protocol and a basic-calculation probe test (§2A–2B).
- **Diagnostic precision layer (§2C):** distractor rules, trap table for choosing numbers, diagnostic item formats (reasoned options, two-tier, confidence, find-the-error, staged open questions, matched pairs), evidence rules and target-code planning.
- **MCQ seed ≠ ready-to-use MCQ**: generate A–D options from the signatures recomputed on the item's numbers: exactly one correct, three distinct errors, no masking (§2C.2). **(v8)** Only rows with `allowed_items` = `MCQ+OPEN` (three or more distinct MCQ-seed signatures) give an MCQ; the `OPEN_ONLY` row gives open items.
- **Open seed ≠ final student question**: rewrite a clear prompt, a full solution *Data → Method → Steps → Result → Check*, and a staged rubric whose criteria carry error codes (§2C.5).
- D1 = automatic fact/process, D2 = standard application, D3 = analysis or proof. The difficulty and time tags are **planning defaults** to be recalibrated using students' real results, not official exam allocations.
- The prompt chooses *some* skills per test; do not confuse coverage of the chosen blueprint with testing every one of the 600 micro-skills during a 55-minute sitting: coverage is always computed on the selected blueprint (§2C.7).

### 1.1 Programme edition and exam-format routing — mandatory

| Track | Use by school year / examination session | Non-negotiable exam-format distinctions |
|---|---|---|
| `BREVET` | In 2026–27 and 2027–28, the **3e** still follows the outgoing cycle 4 programme; new cycle 4 programme applies in **3e from 2028–29** (normally DNB 2029). | [DNB 2027 rules](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/2026/Hebdo4/MENE2623228N): 2h, 20 min / 6 pts no calculator and 1h40 / 14 pts with calculator. **MCQs are not authorised in official Part 2**; diagnostic MCQs remain possible outside exam simulation. |
| `BAC_1RE_SPE`, `BAC_1RE_COMMUN` | New programmes apply **starting school year 2026–27**; for the earlier exam sitting in 2025–26 use the programme in force then. | [From 2026–27, session 2028](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/2026/Special4/MENE2622640N): 2h; 6/14 points; no calculator; **short responses for automatisms, not an MCQ-only section**; includes 2/20 language criteria. MCQs are a diagnostic format, not a claim about the actual paper. |
| `BAC_TLE_SPE`, `BAC_TLE_COMPLEMENTAIRES` | New programmes apply **starting school year 2027–28**. School year 2026–27 uses previous editions. | Confirm current Terminale exam rules and optional assessment by session. Never apply the new school-year content retroactively. |
| `BAC_TLE_EXPERTES` | Check the option edition in force in the target year and its specialist prerequisites. | Optional subject does not have the same independent terminal written exam as specialty mathematics. |
| `MP2I`, `MPI` | MP2I mathematics annex (2021), MPI mathematics annex (2021); check subsequent amendments before generation. | Colles, DS, concours—not a single standardised nationally fixed MCQ format. |

**Critical generator switch:** prompt v4 has `{ASSESSMENT_MODE}` (§0): `DIAGNOSTIC` (MCQ and open as requested, diagnostic options allowed), `EXAM_SIMULATION` (respect the authentic format; no « Je ne sais pas » or confidence boxes) or `TRAINING`. *(v5 recommended this switch as an optional addition to the prompt.)*

### 1.2 Known conflicts, corrections, review obligations

1. **MP2I catalogue mismatch:** the prompt lists `M1-AL` as including determinants, but the source reference separates `M1-DT` (symmetric group and determinants); the reference also adds `M1-F2` (two-variable functions), absent from the prompt. Add these codes to the prompt catalogue if they pass the relevant official scope gate. Until then, do not permit them as standalone `{SUBJECT}` codes without an explicit extension. **(v6: prompt v4 lists `M1-DT` in the catalogue, under the MP2I [TO VERIFY] status, and `M1-F2` on `HOLD`.) (v7: prompt v5 lists both; `M1-F2` eligibility is set per row, `IN-PROG-2021`, item 2.)**
2. **First-year restrictions:** `ℤ/nℤ` **ring structure** is excluded from first-year MP2I; probability is on finite spaces. **(v7)** `M1-F2` is in the 2021 MP2I programme (chapter « Fonctions de deux variables », R24: partial derivatives, class C1, first-order expansion, gradient, derivative along a vector, chain rule, critical points); eligibility is checked per row, with no blanket hold. Continuity of functions of two variables is only a tool there; the Hessian stays an MPI notion. Laplace transforms and second-year-only ratio test are not first-year learning objectives.
3. **First-year common maths 2026 (corrected in v7; v7 correction withdrawn in v9, see item 32):** the 2026 programme of « mathématiques intégrées à l'enseignement scientifique » (R4, read in an academy copy) has a part « variation instantanée et globale »: derivative number, tangent and slope, usual derivatives, variations of polynomials of degree ≤ 3 (already in the 2022 text, R31). The v5–v6 instruction to remove derivative items from `1C-FN` was wrong: `1C-FN-05`…`10` are derivative rows within that scope. new official emphasis is on affine evolution, factorised quadratic, arithmetic/geometric evolution, and positive-base exponential modelling. For quadratics, **do not require the discriminant** in this common track.
4. **MPI:** keep 12 official second-year blocks; Hermitian inner products, proof of d’Alembert–Gauss, proof of the dominated convergence theorem, implicit function theorem and *second differential* are excluded. **The Hessian and its use for second-order optimisation are allowed** in this programme; do not ban them accidentally.
5. **Programme eligibility ≠ diagnostic utility:** a bridge or prerequisite item may be helpful, but must be labelled `PREREQUISITE` rather than falsely claimed to belong to the target year’s explicit programme.
6. **Prompt feasibility inconsistency:** 16 MCQs × 2 min + 3 open questions × 8 min = **56 min**, exceeding 90% of a 55 min test (49.5 min). An alternative is **12 MCQs + 3 open** at those reference times (=48 min), subject to difficulty mix. Do not force an infeasible blueprint.
7. **Misconception evidence:** each `E1/E2/E3` below is a proposed diagnostic error pattern. The prompt asks for “documented misconceptions”; source documentation must be found or the bank explicitly marked as hypothesised. Do not fabricate research citations. **(v6: prompt v4 calls an error "documented" only when §2A cites DEPP 2025 for it.)**
8. **Brevet geometry edition split (v5):** DNB 2027–2028 follow the outgoing cycle 4 programme (similar triangles, homothety, no vectors); the new programme reaches 3e in 2028–29 (converse/contrapositive logic, midline theorems, vectors). Demonstration rows carry an edition tag (`BOTH`, `OUTGOING`, `NEW`).
9. **ε in Terminale (v5):** the 2019 programme in force in 2026–27 defines limits with intervals and states that ∀/∃ are not exigible and that full formalism is not expected. ε–N and ε–δ rows are tagged `BRIDGE → MP2I` and excluded from Terminale diagnostics by default; the 2027–28 programme's formalism level is `HOLD`.
10. **Documented vs hypothesised errors (v5):** 14 transversal `CB` codes (one more partly) are documented by the DEPP 2025 start-of-Seconde automatisms test, with national rates as reported; every other `CB`/`RP` code and every `E1`–`E3` remains a hypothesis until confirmed on students' scripts.
11. **Distractor precision (v6):** v5 errors were labels; v6 gives each one its signature on the seed. 39 seeds were changed because an error produced the key (masking) or two errors produced the same value (collision); each change is noted in its row and the patterns are listed in §2C.3.
12. **Open targets (v6):** all 161 open targets left as "To be worked and independently verified" are now computed; numeric keys, targets and signatures were recomputed by script (§15).
13. **Guardrails applied (v6):** `M1-ST-02` used ℤ/3ℤ against the v5 MP2I guardrail (rewritten with n ↦ iⁿ); `M2-SEN-04` needed a semi-convergence study, excluded by the v5 MPI limits (moved to x = 1); `M1-AA-03` had the limit point written as its key (fixed: 1/2).
14. **Summary docx v2 superseded for generation:** its Première statement "Part 1 — Automatisms MCQ" predates the session-2028 rule of §1.1 (short responses, not an MCQ-only section), and its `1C-FN` derivative line must be read with item 32 (v9: derivatives are not in the 2026 scope). Use this file; the docx lines survive here as "Original" extracts.
15. **Codebook alignment (v6):** the 9 codes proposed in codebook v5.1 (`CB-FRA-08`…`13`, `CB-NUM-06`…`07`, `CB-SGN-10`) are now part of §2A; the 18 v6 codes (`CB-DER-*`, `CB-PRB-*`, `CB-LIM-*`, `RP-13`) must be added to the codebook (status `proposed`) or declared in the quiz `spec.codes`.
16. **Timing (item 6) resolved in prompt v4:** default 12 MCQ + 3 open; the prompt computes the time from the actual difficulty mix and formats.
17. **Codex review of v6 — lineage (v7):** Codex compared v6 with a CODEX v5 that holds 301 targets and 741 `E1`–`E3` IDs, with IDs such as `BR-NC-F*` and `TS-AN-S09` (ε–δ for an affine function). The v5 file used to build v6 — identical to the project document `claude/French_Math_Diagnostic_Curriculum_Reference_CODEX_v5.md` — holds 269 targets and 807 `E1`–`E3` IDs and no `-F` or `-S` IDs; v6 kept all of them with their v5 meanings (807 of 807 labels unchanged). The 32 targets reported missing therefore come from another file, not received for this build. Patterns `SUBDOMAIN-F\d\d` and `SUBDOMAIN-S\d\d` are **reserved**: when that file is supplied, its targets are restored with their own IDs and mapped in the migration table. Candidate equivalent, unconfirmed: `TS-AN-S09` ↔ `TS-AN-17`.
18. **Foundation skills (v7):** 24 new basic-calculation rows `BR-NC-06`…`29` (signed numbers, fractions, powers, roots, literal calculation, equations), with standard IDs, give the independently assessable foundation targets Codex asked for.
19. **Collisions and masking fixed (v7):** `TS-AN-04` (MCQ seed changed: E1 and E3 both gave 2e − 1); `1C-AU-04` (`REASONED`: "−10 % taken on 80 €" and "percentages added" always give the same value); `M1-F2-02` (`REASONED`: E2 gave the right point for a wrong reason; padding error E4 retired); `BR-DF-03` (open question staged with the intermediate price, so that E1 and E3 leave different traces); `BR-AP-01` (E1 and E3 are one rule with one value: declared ambiguous, never both in one item); `M1-AA-03` (`REASONED`: E1 and E3 both gave 0). Five errors have the key's value in another form (`BR-NC-02-E2`, `BR-NC-03-E3`, `M1-AA-02-E1`, `M1-PO-03-E4`, `M2-ED-03-E1`): such options are compared as written, not by value (`form_distinct` in the CSV). The validator scans the 600 rows for masking and collisions (§15).
20. **Unsupported diagnoses (v7):** a value shared by two errors, or a lost rubric point with no visible step, gives `UNDETERMINED` evidence for every candidate code until written work or a follow-up separates them (§2C.6a).
21. **Official anchors (v7):** every row cites the official capability it exercises (`ref`, Appendix A). Each index entry has a verification status: `OFFICIAL` (read in the official PDF in this build), `COPY` (read in an academy, publisher or teacher copy quoting the programme, because the BO page could not be fetched) or `TOC` (chapter title from the official table of contents). `COPY` and `TOC` anchors must be re-checked against the BO before release.
22. **Not indexed (v7):** the Terminale programmes that start in 2027–28 (R6, R7) and the expertes text that will follow; rows are anchored to the 2019 texts in force in 2026–27 (R26–R28) and must be re-anchored before 2027–28.
23. **Reading by seed (v8; Codex AUD-005, AUD-006, AUD-007 withdrawn; AUD-014; Grok rule R1):** the three "signature answers the wrong item" findings (`BR-NC-03-E1`, `M1-PO-03-E1`/`E2`, `M1-AA-02-E3`) compared open-seed signatures (prefix `open:`) with the MCQ seed. They fit the open seeds — GCD(84, 36), X⁴ + 1 over ℂ, the order-3 expansion of sin x — and are not defects; Codex withdrew them and Grok confirmed. To prevent the misreading, every errors cell is split into **MCQ seed:** and **Open seed:** groups, and the CSV gives each error `mode` (`mcq`, `open`, `mcq+open`), `sig_mcq` and `sig_open`. Rule R1 (§2C.1): a signature is compared only with its own seed.
24. **Three distinct MCQ distractors (v8; AUD-003, MCQ-001…084):** a row gives an MCQ only if its MCQ seed carries at least three distinct signatures (`allowed_items` = `MCQ+OPEN`); otherwise it is `OPEN_ONLY`. The v7 scan flagged 84 rows; it exempted `REASONED` rows, 11 of which had only two wrong reasons. v8 adds 107 errors on 94 rows (`E4`–`E6`, marked *(v8)*, each with its signature on the MCQ seed; a wrong reason for a `REASONED` row); `TC-IN-02` is `OPEN_ONLY` (on f(x) = x the midpoint, trapezoid and exact values coincide); `TS-AN-14-E1` is now used on the open seed only (on the MCQ seed, 2.0247 is too close to the key 2.025). A `LOCAL` or unrelated code is never used to reach four options.
25. **`BR-AP-01` (v8; AUD-004, AUD-009):** `E3` ("addition before multiplication") is merged into `E1` ("reverse operation order"): on ×3 then +4 they are one rule with one value. `E3` is never reused and logs coded `E3` read as `E1` (migration `MERGED`). No declared ambiguous pair remains in the catalogue.
26. **Required form (v8; AUD-008):** the MCQ stems of `BR-NC-02` (scientific notation a × 10ⁿ, 1 ≤ a < 10), `BR-NC-03` (product of prime factors), `M1-AA-02` (a + bx + cx² + o(x²)) and `M1-PO-03` (irreducible factors in ℝ[X]) now state the form, because one distractor has the key's value in another form (`form_distinct`, compared as written, marked in the errors cell). `M2-ED-03-E1` (P⁻¹e^(tD)P) was wrongly declared `form_distinct` in v7: matrix products do not commute, so it is a different matrix; the row is compared as written (`compare` = `text`) and its stem names D diagonal and P invertible.
27. **Provisional anchors (v8; AUD-002, SRC rows):** 158 rows have at least one `COPY` or `TOC` anchor (1S 79, 1C 40, TS 1, MP2I 38): `anchor_status` = `PROVISIONAL`, marked in the row head and excluded from verified counts (442 rows `VERIFIED`). The official 2026 Première annexes (R32, R33) could not be read in this build: the BO n° 14 PDF yields its table of contents only (R34) and the annex files are not reachable from this workspace. `Anchor_Verification_Worklist_v8.csv` lists each row, its provisional anchors, the official document and the copy used, with blank columns for the check.
28. **Lineage (v8; AUD-001, AUD-013 — still blocked):** the CODEX v5 used to build v6 (SHA-256 `525c9713…e4fd`, 257 820 bytes) states 269 micro-skills; the 301-target file Codex measured has not been received. Reserved patterns `SUBDOMAIN-F\d\d` and `SUBDOMAIN-S\d\d` stay unused; no target is "restored" from them.
29. **Validator scope (v8; AUD-010):** a run with 0 blocking findings establishes structure (every row re-rendered from the CSV), ID grammar and uniqueness, defined codes, anchor presence and status consistency, migration coverage, the three-distractor rule, masking and collision scans on each seed, and numeric recomputation. It does not establish pedagogical plausibility, that a BO paraphrase supports the capability, empirical diagnostic validity, or YAML compatibility with the player's JSON schema (§15).
30. **Hypotheses (v8; AUD-011):** no pilot response data was available: every signature, old or new, remains a hypothesis, and observations stay `SUSPECTED` until confirmed (§2C.6a).
31. **Terminale 2027–28 (v8; AUD-012):** unchanged (item 22): re-anchor the Terminale rows before 2027–28.
32. **Première common track, derivation (v9; CODEX + GROK G01, P0-01 — reverses item 3):** the official 2026 programme (R33) does not contain the derivative part of the 2022 text: Codex read the official annex, and a union report on the consultation states that the « dérivation » part was removed (R36). The academy copy used in v7 (R30) carried the 2022 content in its « suppressions » section, which v7 misread. `1C-FN-05`…`10` are therefore `HOLD`, anchor `NOT_SUPPORTED`, excluded from `BAC_1RE_COMMUN` assessments; their IDs and history are kept, and they may be used only as optional bridge tasks. The index entries `1C.FN.3` and `1C.FN.4` are `NOT_SUPPORTED`.
33. **Original CODEX v5 lineage (v9; G02, P0-02):** the 301-target CODEX v5 has been received (SHA-256 `f7e4fd98…8ad2`, R37). None of its 301 IDs (`-F##`, `-S##`) is an ID of this reference. All 301 are enumerated in `Math_ID_Migration_to_v9.csv` (`legacy_version` = `CODEX_v5_301`) with a proposed candidate (274 `EXACT` or `PARTIAL` proposals; 27 `UNMAPPED`): same subdomain and number when the capability text matches, else the closest capability; the 24 foundation targets `BR-NC-F01`…`F24` by a manual topic match; every entry is `PROPOSED_UNREVIEWED`. The 27 legacy `EC-*` codes are listed in `Legacy_EC_Codes_CODEX_v5.csv` as `LEGACY_ONLY`: none is mapped to a `CB-*` code by similar spelling. Migration is **not** complete until a reviewer sets each relation (`EXACT`, `PARTIAL`, `SPLIT`, `MERGE`, `NOT_EQUIVALENT`, `UNMAPPED`).
34. **Anchor vocabulary (v9; G01, P1-05):** `SOURCE_LINKED` (442 rows; v8 `VERIFIED`) means every anchor is an index entry read in an official document — not that each capability was reviewed by a teacher; `PROVISIONAL` (152) means at least one anchor was read in a copy or a table of contents; `NOT_SUPPORTED` (6) means the official programme does not support the row. `CAPABILITY_VERIFIED` is reserved for rows whose capability a reviewer has matched, clause by clause, in `Anchor_Verification_Worklist_v9.csv` (none yet).
35. **Release gates (v9; G03, G09, G10, P0-03):** `STRUCTURE_PASS`, `CALCULATION_PASS`, `CURRICULUM_PASS`, `LEGACY_MIGRATION_PASS`, `PLAYER_SCHEMA_PASS` (or `NOT_APPLICABLE` for paper), `TEACHER_REVIEW_PASS`. `RELEASE_READY` needs all six for the release profile. **Zero blocking findings establishes only a structural pass. Programme passages, mathematical coverage and teacher review remain required for any release labelled "validated". Today 152 rows are `PROVISIONAL` and 6 `NOT_SUPPORTED` (158 not source-linked).**
36. **Calculation coverage (v9; G06, P1-02):** the validator reports how many rows and error IDs its configured checks reach, not only the number of assertions. The two `+∞` signatures (`M1-SN-03-E5`, `M2-IN-04-E5`) are in `chk_ADD.py` and pass in v8 and v9; the report now lists symbolic signatures and their check status. The 310 v6 checks are labelled `STATIC_LEGACY_SANITY`: they recompute values but do not read the current CSV.
37. **`BR-AP-01-E3` (v9; G07, P1-04):** the merge into `E1` stands (the two rules are the same transformation, 3(n + 4) on n → 3n → 3n + 4). Raw student logs keep `BR-AP-01-E3`; reports aggregate it under `BR-AP-01-E1` through the migration table, and never count both as two errors.
38. **Pilot and terminology (v9; G04, P1-03):** each error has `research_status` = `HYPOTHESIS`; `PILOT_SUPPORTED` needs coded pilot scripts (a first feasibility pilot of 20–30 pseudonymised scripts per track, second coding on a subset), and `RESEARCH_DOCUMENTED` an identified source. Two selected distractors alone do not confirm a mechanism without an explanation or a discriminating follow-up item.
39. **M2-SF-04 reproducibility (v10; audit A05, P0):** Grok's fresh run reported one failure on the series check of `M2-SF-04`, both values printing as 1.38629436111989. Cause: v9's `check_values_v6.py` compared two SymPy `Float`s with exact symbolic equality (`simplify(a − b) == 0`), which depends on the platform's last bit; the 80-term partial sum and 2 ln 2 can differ by one unit in the last place. Correction: (1) any value carrying a `Float` is an approximation and is compared with a relative tolerance of 10⁻⁹ (exact symbolic values stay exact); (2) the `M2-SF-04` series check now sums the 80 terms as exact rationals and checks |2 ln 2 − S₈₀| < 3 × 10⁻²⁶, a proven bound (tail < 2⁻⁷⁹/81 ≈ 2.04 × 10⁻²⁶). The report records Python, SymPy and platform, so two runs can be compared.
40. **v6 checks linked to the CSV (v10; A02):** the 310 v6 checks now run inside the validator; a check counts as coverage only when the value it confirms equals the value shown in the current CSV (`v6_linked_to_csv`); mismatches are listed for review, not hidden.
41. **Item-level release eligibility (v10; A04, P0):** `Item_Release_Eligibility_v10.csv` gives, for each of the 600 rows, structure, calculation, curriculum, player schema, teacher review and diagnostic validity, then `eligibility_paper` and `eligibility_digital` (`PASS` / `HOLD` / `FAIL`) with the reasons. Reviews are recorded in `Item_Review_Register_v10.csv` (BO page and passage, reviewer, date; teacher decision; text signatures; pilot status). A quiz may use only `PASS` rows for a validated release.
42. **Curriculum gate (v10; A01, P0):** `SOURCE_LINKED` means the official source is identified; it does not pass the curriculum gate. A row passes only when the register records `bo_match` = `SUPPORTED` with a reviewer and a date. `NOT_SUPPORTED` and `HOLD` rows fail.
43. **Seventh gate (v10; A08):** `DIAGNOSTIC_VALIDITY_PASS` stays `HOLD` while the error patterns of the rows in scope are `HYPOTHESIS`; it needs pilot evidence (item 38).
44. **Curriculum evidence enforced (v11; CODEX + Grok P0-01):** a row passes the curriculum gate only with `bo_match` = `SUPPORTED`, a non-empty `bo_page` (or stable section) and `bo_passage`, a reviewer, and a `bo_reviewed_on` date written YYYY-MM-DD; the reason lists each missing field.
45. **Text signatures (v11; P0-02):** a row's text signatures are cleared only by `text_signatures_reviewed` = `YES` with `text_reviewer`, `text_reviewed_on` and `text_review_note`; this clears the text part only — an unchecked key or numeric signature still holds the row.
46. **Diagnostic validity (v11; P0-03):** positive evidence only: every active error must be `PILOT_SUPPORTED` or `RESEARCH_DOCUMENTED`, and the register must give `pilot_status` = `PILOTED`, `pilot_evidence_id` and `pilot_reviewed_on`. A blank or unknown status never passes.
47. **Paper and digital readiness (v11; P0-04):** `eligibility_paper` ignores the player; `eligibility_digital` needs `Player_Validation_Record_v11.json` (schema file and SHA-256, sample quiz, schema valid, player smoke test PASS, tester, date). `release_status` is given for paper and digital separately, with its scope (all 600 rows).
48. **Teacher review (v11; P0-05):** `APPROVED` needs a reviewer and a `teacher_reviewed_on` date.
49. **Rebuild across Python versions (v11; P0-06):** the v10 Markdown embedded the Python version of the run, so a rebuild on Python 3.13.5 differed from the published file (3.13.18). Runtime provenance now goes to `validation_report_v11.json` only; the generated Markdown is deterministic. Grok's validation on Python 3.10.21 / SymPy 1.14.0 and CODEX's on 3.13.5 are recorded as independent runs.
50. **Exact numbers and coverage (v11; P1-1 to P1-4):** in `check_values_v6.py`, integers and fractions are compared exactly (10¹⁸ and 10¹⁸ + 1 differ), approximate values with a relative tolerance of 10⁻⁹ and an absolute tolerance of 10⁻¹² (0 and 5 × 10⁻¹⁰ differ); unreachable code was removed. `Coverage_Gaps_v11.csv` lists each unchecked key, numeric signature, infinity and text signature, and the v6 checks of intermediate values. Counts are stated as 152 `PROVISIONAL` + 6 `NOT_SUPPORTED` (158 not source-linked). The 301 CODEX v5 IDs and their source are inside the package: what remains is their review, not the file.

## 2. Machine-readable skill identification and authoring contract

One row = one testable operation; the identifier grammar is `SUBDOMAIN-NN`, e.g. `M2-SF-01`. Error IDs are `SUBDOMAIN-NN-E1`, `-E2`, `-E3`. Always retain the parent code for backwards compatibility with the supplied prompt. **(v7)** An ID is never reused for another skill or error: retired IDs stay retired, and the patterns `SUBDOMAIN-F\d\d` and `SUBDOMAIN-S\d\d` are reserved (§1.2 item 17, Appendix B).

```yaml
micro_skill:
  id: "BR-DF-04"
  level: "BREVET"
  subdomain: "BR-DF"
  capability: "Find an antecedent under an affine function"
  official_edition: "cycle 4, outgoing edition (DNB 2027–2028), to recheck at generation"
  official_ref: "BR.DF.9"                      # v7: Appendix A (verified OFFICIAL | COPY | TOC)
  eligibility: "IN-PROG"                       # v7: IN-PROG (Brevet, with edition) | IN-PROG-2019 | IN-PROG-2021 | IN-PROG-2026 | PREREQUISITE | BRIDGE | HOLD
  prerequisite: ["BR-NC"]
  difficulty: 2
  mcq_format: "SINGLE_KEY"                     # v6: SINGLE_KEY | REASONED | TWO_TIER
  mcq_seed: "f(x) = 3x − 2. Antecedent of 7?"
  answer_seed: "3"
  errors:                                      # v6: one object per error; E4+ are v6 additions
    - {id: "BR-DF-04-E1", rule: "image computed instead of antecedent: f(c)", signature: "19", type: READ, tags: ["CB-VER-04"]}
    - {id: "BR-DF-04-E2", rule: "x = (c − b) × a instead of (c − b)/a", signature: "27", type: CONC, tags: ["CB-EQ-01"]}
    - {id: "BR-DF-04-E3", rule: "constant term ignored: x = c/a", signature: "7/3", type: PROC, tags: []}
    - {id: "BR-DF-04-E4", rule: "b moved without sign change: x = (c + b)/a", signature: "5/3", type: PROC, tags: ["CB-SGN-05"]}
                                               # v7: optional ambiguous_with: [error IDs with the same signature]
  edition: "BOTH"                              # BOTH | OUTGOING | NEW (Brevet); 2019 | 2027 (Terminale)
  open_seed: "A taxi fare is 4 + 2x euros for x km. Find x when the fare is 18 €."
  open_target: "x = 7 km"
  solution_scheme: ["Data", "Method", "Steps", "Result", "Check"]
  marking_rubric_points: 4
  validation: ["scope", "answer", "signatures distinct", "no masking", "notation", "time"]
```

**Ready-to-use generation constraints**

- MCQs must give four distinct choices (A–D), exactly one correct, and three **different, plausible errors**. Avoid a distractor identical to the key due to simplification, rounding, or equivalent interval notation. Test every choice with symbolic or independent manual calculation.
- Open answers must accept mathematically equivalent forms and proofs. A full solution must be computed from the stated example; the target in the source row is not an automatically verified official answer.
- For each MCQ, emit the mapping `A/B/C/D → correct or misconception ID`. Never expose keys or misconceptions in the student version.
- For each open item, emit a four-part rubric (usually 1 pt data/domain, 1 pt method, 1 pt execution, 1 pt conclusion/check); for D3 proofs, reallocate points to hypotheses, quantifiers and logical justification.
- Respect calculator restrictions; avoid logarithmic approximations without a calculator unless an exact form is accepted.
- If a source status is `HOLD`, `REVIEW`, or session-mismatched, the generator **must first verify** the exact capacity in the relevant BO annex. Exclude on failed or unavailable verification. Do not silently replace with a neighbouring notion.
- **(v5)** Tag every distractor and every rubric step with its micro-skill error ID **and** 0–2 transversal codes (§2A); prefer DEPP-documented `CB` codes for D1 distractors. `BRIDGE` rows enter a diagnostic only when the learner profile targets the next level's prerequisites.
- **(v6)** Build every distractor by applying an error rule to the item's own numbers and recompute its signature; run the S1–S7 checks of §2C.2 (distinct after normalisation, no masking, single cause, positions). Write binary seeds as `REASONED` options. Give each open item staged parts with expected intermediate results and rubric criteria that carry error codes (§2C.5).

## 2A. Transversal error bank — basic calculation (`CB`) and reasoning/proof (`RP`) — v5, extended in v6

Micro-skill error IDs (`SUBDOMAIN-NN-E1…E3`) say **where** a student failed; transversal codes say **which basic mechanism** failed. The same mechanism (a sign in front of brackets, a fraction added term by term, a quantifier in the wrong order) reappears from the Brevet to MPI, so a student's errors can only be followed over time if both are recorded. Tag every lost point with its micro-skill error ID **and** 0–2 transversal codes.

- **Code grammar:** `CB-<FAMILY>-NN` = *calcul de base*; `RP-NN` = *raisonnement et preuve*. Codes are stable: never renumber, only add.
- **(v6) Content:** 97 `CB` codes in 15 families and 13 `RP` codes: the v5 bank, the 9 codes proposed in codebook v5.1 (marked "codebook v5.1") and 18 new codes (marked "v6": families `CB-DER`, `CB-PRB`, `CB-LIM`, and `RP-13`).
- **Evidence:** `DEPP 2025` = error documented in the national start-of-Seconde automatisms test 2025 (DEPP, *document de travail* 2025-E15, January 2026; national rates quoted as reported in that document — re-check before publishing them). `hyp.` = design hypothesis, to be confirmed on students' scripts (same rule as §1.2, item 7).
- **Type:** `SLIP` (inattention; self-corrected when the line is pointed out) · `PROC` (wrong rule or procedure) · `CONC` (misconception about the object) · `NOTA` (notation or writing) · `READ` (statement, graph or scale misread) · `CHECK` (no plausibility or substitution check).
- **Levels:** where the error is still typically observed (BR = Brevet, 2nde, 1re, Tle, MP2I, MPI).
- Student-facing versions use the French decimal comma; this reference uses the decimal point.

### 2A.1 Families and default remediation

| Family | Scope | Codes | Default remediation sequence (error → reminder → guided → transfer → re-probe) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-SGN` | Signs and relative numbers | 01–10 | Number line / gains–losses model → 10 quick sign items → sign inside an algebraic step → probe after 1–2 weeks |
| `CB-PAR` | Brackets, priorities, substitution | 01–06 | Rewrite with explicit brackets, draw the distribution arrows → 6 "every term" items → substitution of negative values → probe |
| `CB-FRA` | Fractions | 01–13 | Area or bar model of a common denominator; rule "simplify factors, never terms" → 8 graded items → algebraic fractions → probe |
| `CB-PUI` | Powers, roots, scientific notation, exp/ln | 01–08 | Rebuild each rule from its meaning (10³ = 10 × 10 × 10) → numerical counterexample check (√(9 + 16) ≠ 3 + 4) → probe |
| `CB-LIT` | Literal calculation | 01–05 | Test an identity by substituting x = 1 and x = 2 → 6 expand/factorise pairs → probe |
| `CB-EQ` | Equations and solution sets | 01–07 | Balance model, "same operation on the whole side", systematic substitution check → probe |
| `CB-INQ` | Inequalities and absolute value (needed for ε work) | 01–05 | Number-line reading of \|x − a\| < r; test with numbers before generalising → probe |
| `CB-NUM` | Decimals, rounding, orders of magnitude | 01–07 | Place-value table; estimate before computing → probe |
| `CB-PCT` | Percentages, proportionality, scale | 01–06 | Multiplier coefficient 1 ± p/100 used systematically; ratio tables → probe |
| `CB-UNI` | Units and conversions | 01–04 | Conversion tables with squared/cubed steps; a unit on every line → probe |
| `CB-NOT` | Notation and mathematical writing | 01–05 | One equality per line; arrows only for logical links; index tables (n, uₙ) → probe |
| `CB-VER` | Checking and reading | 01–04 | End-of-exercise checklist (order of magnitude, sign, unit, substitution) → probe |
| `CB-DER` (v6) | Derivatives and primitives | 01–05 | Rebuild each rule on a known case ((x²)′ = 2x; check by differentiating the primitive) → 6 items mixing products and compositions → ODE with initial condition → probe |
| `CB-PRB` (v6) | Probability: conditioning, trees, counting, variance | 01–09 | Tree or two-way table drawn every time; read "probability of … given …" aloud; counts out of 1 000 people → graded items → probe |
| `CB-LIM` (v6) | Limits, indeterminate forms, equivalents | 01–03 | Factor the dominant term; list of forms that are not rules (∞/∞, 0/0, ∞ − ∞, 1^∞); expansions instead of sums of equivalents → probe |
| `RP` | Reasoning and proof | 01–13 | Model proofs ("On sait que / Or / Donc"), spot-the-error tasks, counterexample hunts → probe |

### 2A.2 Basic-calculation codes

#### `CB-SGN` — signs and relative numbers

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-SGN-01` | Sign rule misapplied in a product or quotient, or across the intervals of a sign table | (−3) × (−4) × (−1) = 12 → −12 | (−2) × (−5) × (−1) **[−10]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-SGN-02` | Subtracting a negative number treated as subtracting a positive one | 5 − (−3) = 2 → 8 | −4 − (−7) **[3]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → 2nde |
| `CB-SGN-03` | Minus sign in front of brackets not applied to every term | −(2x − 3) = −2x − 3 → −2x + 3 | Expand −(3 − x) **[−3 + x]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-SGN-04` | −a² confused with (−a)² | −3² = 9 → −9 | −2² and (−2)² **[−4 ; 4]** | NOTA | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-SGN-05` | Sign lost when a term changes side | x + 5 = 2 ⇒ x = 7 → x = −3 | Solve x + 5 = 2 **[x = −3]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → 2nde |
| `CB-SGN-06` | Inequality not reversed when multiplying or dividing by a negative number | −2x < 6 ⇒ x < −3 → x > −3 | Solve −3x > 12 **[x < −4]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-SGN-07` | Signed numbers added by adding absolute values, or the wrong sign kept or dropped | −7 + 3 = −10 or 4 → −4 | −7 + 3 **[−4]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-SGN-08` | "−x" read as "a negative number" | "−x > 0 is impossible" → x < 0 | If −x > 0, what is the sign of x? **[x < 0]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q9:** 38.0 % correct; 25.1 % "cannot say", 18.7 % "x positive", 15.7 % "x = 0" | BR → Tle |
| `CB-SGN-09` | Value a read with the wrong sign in an (x − a) form (roots, vertex, centre) | (x + 3)(x − 1) = 0 ⇒ x = 3 or −1 → x = −3 or 1 | Solutions of (x + 3)(x − 1) = 0 **[−3 ; 1]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-SGN-10` | Negative decimals ordered like positives, or by distance to zero | −0.9 < −2.5 → −2.5 < −0.9 | Order −1.2 ; −1.05 ; −3 **[−3 < −1.2 < −1.05]** | CONC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → 2nde |

#### `CB-PAR` — brackets, priorities, substitution

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-PAR-01` | Operations performed left to right, priorities ignored | 2 + 3 × 4 = 20 → 14 | 7 + 3 × 5 **[22]** | PROC | hyp. | BR |
| `CB-PAR-02` | Factor distributed to the first term only | 3(5x + 1) = 15x + 1 → 15x + 3 | Expand 4(x − 3) **[4x − 12]** | PROC | **DEPP 2025 Q5:** 74.0 % correct; 20.3 % answered 15x + 1 | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PAR-03` | Negative or compound value substituted without brackets | x = −3: x² − 2x = −9 + 6 → 9 + 6 = 15 | x² − 3x for x = −2 **[10]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-PAR-04` | Power applied to one factor only | (3x)² = 3x² → 9x² | (2x)³ **[8x³]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PAR-05` | Fraction bar not treated as brackets | (6 + 4)/2 = 6 + 2 → 5 | (8 + 4)/4 **[3]** | NOTA | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PAR-06` | Double distribution with missing cross terms | (x + 2)(x + 3) = x² + 6 → x² + 5x + 6 | Expand (x + 1)(x − 4) **[x² − 3x − 4]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |

#### `CB-FRA` — fractions

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-FRA-01` | Numerators and denominators added or subtracted separately | 4/5 − 1/3 = 3/2 → 7/15 | 1/4 + 2/3 **[11/12]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q8:** 63.9 % correct; 23.3 % answered 3/2 | BR → Tle |
| `CB-FRA-02` | Product of fractions done by cross-products or after reducing to a common denominator | 2/5 × 2/3 = 6/10 or 60/15 → 4/15 | 3/4 × 2/5 **[3/10]** | PROC | **DEPP 2025 Q3:** 84.2 % correct; 7.6 % 6/10; 6.3 % 60/15 | BR → 2nde |
| `CB-FRA-03` | Division by a fraction not done by multiplying by its reciprocal | 2/3 ÷ 4/5 = 8/15 → 5/6 | 3/4 ÷ 9/8 **[2/3]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-FRA-04` | Whole number × fraction multiplies both terms | 3 × 2/5 = 6/15 → 6/5 | 4 × 3/7 **[12/7]** | PROC | hyp. | BR |
| `CB-FRA-05` | Terms of a sum cancelled as if they were factors | (x + 3)/3 = x → x/3 + 1 | Simplify (3x + 6)/3 **[x + 2]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-FRA-06` | Exponent of a fraction multiplies its terms | (1/2)² = 2/4 or 1 → 1/4 | (2/3)² **[4/9]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q2:** 49.8 % correct; 37.0 % 2/4; 10.9 % 1 | BR → 2nde |
| `CB-FRA-07` | Algebraic fractions added term by term | 1/x + 1/2 = 2/(x + 2) → (x + 2)/(2x) | 1/x − 1/(x + 1) **[1/(x(x + 1))]** | PROC | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-FRA-08` | Fraction read as a decimal by putting the digits side by side | 3/4 = 3.4 → 0.75 | Decimal form of 2/5 **[0.4]** | CONC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → 2nde |
| `CB-FRA-09` | Fraction inverted (a/b treated as b/a) | 3/4 of 24 = 32 → 18 | 2/5 of 30 **[12]** | CONC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → 2nde |
| `CB-FRA-10` | Fractions compared term by term | 7/12 > 5/6 "since 7 > 5 and 12 > 6" → 7/12 < 10/12 | Compare 3/4 and 5/6 **[3/4 < 5/6]** | CONC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → 2nde |
| `CB-FRA-11` | Fraction of a quantity: only one of the two operations done | 3/4 of 24 = 72 or 6 → 18 | 2/3 of 18 **[12]** | PROC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → BR |
| `CB-FRA-12` | Equal fractions built by adding the same number, or by changing one term only | 6/8 = 5/7; 1/4 = 1/12 → 6/8 = 3/4 | 2/3 = ?/12 **[8]** | CONC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → BR |
| `CB-FRA-13` | Product rule applied to a sum | 2/5 + 1/4 = 2/20 → 13/20 | 1/2 + 1/5 **[7/10]** | PROC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → 1re |

#### `CB-PUI` — powers, roots, scientific notation, exp and ln

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-PUI-01` | Power rules misapplied in products or quotients (exponents multiplied, bases multiplied) | 10⁵ × 10³ = 10¹⁵ or 100⁸ → 10⁸ | 10⁴ × 10² **[10⁶]** | PROC | **DEPP 2025 Q7:** 56.6 % correct; 18.2 % 10¹⁵; 14.5 % 100⁸; 9.9 % 100¹⁵ | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PUI-02` | Power of a power: exponents added | (2³)² = 2⁵ → 2⁶ | (10²)³ **[10⁶]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PUI-03` | Negative exponent read as a negative number or as a product | 10⁻³ = 0.003, −30 or −10³ → 0.001 | 2⁻² **[1/4]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q1:** 73.3 % correct; 16.4 % 0.003; 5.4 % −30; 4.2 % −10³ | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PUI-04` | Square and square root treated as linear | √(9 + 16) = 7 → 5; (a + b)² = a² + b² | √(36 + 64) **[10]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-PUI-05` | Power computed as base × exponent; a⁰ = 0 | 3² = 6; 5⁰ = 0 → 9; 1 | 4³ and 7⁰ **[64 ; 1]** | PROC | partly documented: DEPP 2025 Q2, 10.9 % answered (1/2)² = 1 | BR |
| `CB-PUI-06` | Scientific notation: mantissa outside [1 ; 10[ or exponent shifted the wrong way | 0.0042 = 4.2 × 10³ or 42 × 10⁻⁴ → 4.2 × 10⁻³ | 0.00056 **[5.6 × 10⁻⁴]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-PUI-07` | Sum of equal powers treated as a power of the sum | 2ⁿ + 2ⁿ = 4ⁿ → 2ⁿ⁺¹ | 3ⁿ + 3ⁿ + 3ⁿ **[3ⁿ⁺¹]** | CONC | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-PUI-08` | exp and ln treated as linear | e^(x + 2) = eˣ + e² → e² · eˣ; ln(a + b) = ln a + ln b | ln 2 + ln 5 **[ln 10]** | CONC | hyp. | Tle → MP2I |

#### `CB-LIT` — literal calculation

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-LIT-01` | Unlike terms combined | 2n² + 3n² + 4n + 5 = 9n² + 5 or 14n² → 5n² + 4n + 5 | Reduce 5x + 2 − 3x **[2x + 2]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q10:** 85.1 % correct; 8.2 % 9n² + 5; 3.6 % 14n² | BR → Tle |
| `CB-LIT-02` | x × x = 2x, or x + x = x² | → x² and 2x | x × x + x **[x² + x]** | CONC | hyp. | BR |
| `CB-LIT-03` | Remarkable identities misused (2ab missing, sign of b², a² − b² confused with (a − b)²) | (x − 3)² = x² − 9 → x² − 6x + 9 | Expand (x − 4)² **[x² − 8x + 16]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-LIT-04` | Factorisation incomplete, or the factor 1 lost | x² + x factorised as x(x) → x(x + 1) | Factorise 3x² − 6x **[3x(x − 2)]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-LIT-05` | Common factor not recognised when it is a bracket | (x + 1)² − 3(x + 1) expanded instead of factorised | Factorise (x + 1)² − 3(x + 1) **[(x + 1)(x − 2)]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |

#### `CB-EQ` — equations and solution sets

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-EQ-01` | Opposite and inverse confused when isolating x | −2x = 6 ⇒ x = 6 + 2 or x = 6 × (−2) → x = −3 | Solve −2x = 8 **[x = −4]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q6:** 44.4 % chose the correct explanation; 17.9 %, 20.5 % and 13.4 % chose explanations mixing these operations | BR → 2nde |
| `CB-EQ-02` | Operation applied to one term only | 2x + 4 = 10 ⇒ x + 4 = 5 → x = 3 | Solve 3x + 6 = 15 **[x = 3]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → 2nde |
| `CB-EQ-03` | Incomplete solution set (one root of x² = a, one factor forgotten, division by x) | x² = 3x ⇒ x = 3 → x = 0 or x = 3 | Solve x² = 4x **[0 ; 4]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-EQ-04` | Squaring treated as an equivalence; extraneous root kept | √(x + 2) = x ⇒ x = 2 or −1 → x = 2 | Solve √(x + 6) = x **[x = 3]** | CONC | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-EQ-05` | Zero-product rule used with a non-zero right-hand side | (x − 1)(x + 1) = 3 ⇒ x = 4 or x = 2 → x = 2 or x = −2 | Is "(x − 1)(x + 1) = 3 ⇒ x − 1 = 3 or x + 1 = 3" valid? **[No: x² = 4]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-EQ-06` | Inequation solved as an equation (a single value given) | 2x + 3 ≤ 11 ⇒ x = 4 → x ≤ 4 | Solve 3x − 1 < 8 **[x < 3]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-EQ-07` | Solution-set notation: strict/large, bracket direction, ∞, union vs intersection | x ≤ 4 written [4 ; −∞[ → ]−∞ ; 4] | Write "x > −1 and x ≤ 3" as an interval **[ ]−1 ; 3] ]** | NOTA | hyp. | 2nde → MP2I |

#### `CB-INQ` — inequalities and absolute value

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-INQ-01` | Inequality inverted (reciprocals, square roots) without checking the direction | 1/n < 0.01 ⇒ n < 100 → n > 100 | For n > 0: 1/n < 10⁻³ ⇔ ? **[n > 1000]** | PROC | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-INQ-02` | \|x − a\| < r not translated into a − r < x < a + r (one side lost) | \|x − 2\| < 0.5 ⇒ x < 2.5 → 1.5 < x < 2.5 | \|x − 2\| < 0.5 ⇔ ? **[1.5 < x < 2.5]** | CONC | hyp. (2nde programme: \|x − a\| ≤ r ⇔ x ∈ [a − r ; a + r]) | 2nde → MP2I |
| `CB-INQ-03` | \|a + b\| = \|a\| + \|b\| assumed (triangle inequality ignored or reversed) | \|3 + (−5)\| = 8 → 2, and 2 ≤ 8 | Compare \|−4 + 1\| and \|−4\| + \|1\| **[3 ≤ 5]** | CONC | hyp. | Tle → MP2I |
| `CB-INQ-04` | Inequality squared regardless of signs | −3 < 2 ⇒ 9 < 4 | Is "a < b ⇒ a² < b²" true for a = −3, b = 2? **[No]** | CONC | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-INQ-05` | Inequalities subtracted or divided term by term | a < b and c < d ⇒ a − c < b − d | Test with 1 < 2 and 0 < 5 **[1 − 0 = 1 is not < 2 − 5 = −3]** | CONC | hyp. | Tle → MP2I |

#### `CB-NUM` — decimals, rounding, orders of magnitude

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-NUM-01` | Decimals compared by their number of digits | 0.125 > 0.4 → 0.125 < 0.4 | Order 0.4 ; 0.35 ; 0.125 **[0.125 < 0.35 < 0.4]** | CONC | hyp. | BR |
| `CB-NUM-02` | Decimal point moved the wrong way when multiplying or dividing by 10ⁿ ("add a zero") | 2.5 × 10 = 2.50 → 25 | 0.07 × 100 **[7]** | PROC | hyp. | BR |
| `CB-NUM-03` | "Dividing always makes smaller" (divisor below 1) | 7 ÷ 0.5 = 3.5 → 14 | 6 ÷ 0.2 **[30]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-NUM-04` | Intermediate results rounded too early; false precision | 1000 × 1.05³ computed by rounding each step | 1000 × 1.05³ exactly **[1157.625]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-NUM-05` | Powers of ten ignored when comparing (mantissas compared) | 4.2 × 10⁻⁴ > 3.8 × 10⁻³ → < | Compare 9 × 10⁻⁵ and 1.1 × 10⁻⁴ **[9 × 10⁻⁵ < 1.1 × 10⁻⁴]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-NUM-06` | Number of decimal places miscounted in a product | 0.5 × 4 = 0.2 → 2 | 0.6 × 7 **[4.2]** | PROC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → BR |
| `CB-NUM-07` | Decimal parts added as whole numbers | 2.7 + 1.4 = 3.11 → 4.1 | 3.8 + 2.5 **[6.3]** | PROC | hyp. (codebook v5.1) | 5e → BR |

#### `CB-PCT` — percentages, proportionality, scale

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-PCT-01` | Successive percentages added | +20 % then −20 % = 0 % → −4 % | +10 % then −10 % **[−1 %]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PCT-02` | Reverse percentage computed on the wrong base | 100 € after +25 % ⇒ 75 € → 80 € | A price is 60 € after +20 %. Initial price? **[50 €]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PCT-03` | Additive reasoning instead of multiplicative (proportionality, enlargement, growth) | 3 objects 12 €, so 5 objects 14 € → 20 € | 3 objects cost 12 €; how much for 5? **[20 €]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q12:** 78.4 % correct, 6.6 % additive; **Q13** (reduction of a rectangle): 56.5 % correct, 30.7 % additive | BR → 1re |
| `CB-PCT-04` | Multiplier for +p % written x + p/100, x + p or (1 + p/10)x | +3 %: x + 0.03, x + 3, 1.3x → 1.03x | Multiplier for +7 % **[1.07]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q11:** 29.9 % correct; 40.7 % x + 0.03; 13.8 % x + 3; 12.7 % 1.3x | BR → Tle |
| `CB-PCT-05` | p % taken as p units, or percentage points confused with percent | 10 % → 12 % called "+2 %" → +2 points = +20 % | A rate goes from 20 % to 25 %: relative change? **[+25 %]** | CONC | hyp. | 1re → Tle |
| `CB-PCT-06` | Degrees read as percentages in a pie chart | 90° sector = 90 % → 25 % | A 72° sector is what percentage? **[20 %]** | CONC | **DEPP 2025 Q15:** 75.7 % correct; 12.2 % 45 %; 6.8 % 90 % | BR |

#### `CB-UNI` — units and conversions

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-UNI-01` | Linear factor used for area or volume conversions | 1 m² = 100 cm² → 10 000 cm² | 2.5 m² in cm² **[25 000 cm²]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → 1re |
| `CB-UNI-02` | Decimal hours read as hours and minutes | 1.5 h = 1 h 50 min → 1 h 30 min | 1.25 h **[1 h 15 min]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-UNI-03` | Inconsistent units within one formula (km and m, km/h and min, degrees and radians) | 30 min at 60 km/h = 1 800 km → 30 km | 45 min at 80 km/h **[60 km]** | PROC | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-UNI-04` | Rate and quantity units confused (kW vs kWh), or unit missing in the answer | 2 kW × 3 h = 6 kW → 6 kWh | 2 kW for 3 h at 0.20 €/kWh **[1.20 €]** | CONC | hyp. | BR → Tle |

#### `CB-NOT` — notation and writing

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-NOT-01` | "=" chained between unequal quantities, or "=" used as an assignment | 3 × 4 = 12 + 5 = 17 | Rewrite it correctly **[3 × 4 + 5 = 12 + 5 = 17]** | NOTA | hyp. | BR → Tle |
| `CB-NOT-02` | Arrows ⇒ / ⇔ misused between non-equivalent steps | x² = 4 ⇔ x = 2 → x = 2 or x = −2 | Is "x² = 4 ⇔ x = 2" correct? **[No]** | NOTA | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-NOT-03` | Juxtaposition misread (2x with x = 8 read as 28) | 2x = 28 → 16 | Value of 3x for x = 5 **[15]** | NOTA | **DEPP 2025 Q4:** 1.3 % (rare) | BR |
| `CB-NOT-04` | Function notation treated as a product or as linear | f(x + h) = f(x) + h; f(2x) = 2f(x) | f(x) = x²: f(x + 1)? **[x² + 2x + 1]** | CONC | hyp. | 2nde → MP2I |
| `CB-NOT-05` | Rank and term confused; index shifted (u₍ₙ₊₁₎ = uₙ + 1, off-by-one) | uₙ = 3n − 1: u₍ₙ₊₁₎ = 3n → 3n + 2 | uₙ = 3n − 1: u₍ₙ₊₁₎? **[3n + 2]** | NOTA | hyp. | 1re → MP2I |

#### `CB-VER` — checking and reading

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-VER-01` | Implausible result not detected (negative length, probability > 1, wrong order of magnitude) | P(A) = 1.3 kept | "A student finds P(A) = 1.3." What do you conclude? **[error: 0 ≤ P ≤ 1]** | CHECK | hyp. | all |
| `CB-VER-02` | Solution not checked by substitution | — | Does x = −1 solve x² − x − 2 = 0? **[yes]** | CHECK | hyp. | all |
| `CB-VER-03` | Copying slip between two lines (sign, digit, exponent) | — | observed on scripts only | SLIP | hyp. | all |
| `CB-VER-04` | Statement, graph or scale misread (image vs antecedent, perimeter vs area, axis scale) | antecedent asked, image given | On a graph, read the antecedent of a given value **[an x-value]** | READ | **DEPP 2025 Q17:** 89.2 % correct; 6.0 % read the image | all |

#### `CB-DER` — derivatives and primitives (v6)

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-DER-01` | Product (or quotient) rule replaced by the product (quotient) of the derivatives | (x eˣ)′ = eˣ → (x + 1)eˣ | Derivative of x²eˣ **[(x² + 2x)eˣ]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-DER-02` | Inner derivative forgotten (chain rule, x ↦ g(ax + b)) | (e³ˣ)′ = e³ˣ → 3e³ˣ | Derivative of (2x + 1)³ **[6(2x + 1)²]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MPI |
| `CB-DER-03` | Derivative and primitive confused (differentiates when asked to integrate, or the reverse) | primitive of x² = 2x → x³/3 | A primitive of 3x² **[x³]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MP2I |
| `CB-DER-04` | Sign error in a usual derivative (cos, e⁻ˣ, 1/x) | (cos x)′ = sin x → −sin x | Derivative of cos(2x) **[−2 sin(2x)]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-DER-05` | Integration constant or initial condition lost or misplaced | y′ = 2y, y(0) = 3: y = e²ˣ + 3 → 3e²ˣ | Solve y′ = 2y, y(0) = 3 **[y = 3e²ˣ]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MPI |

#### `CB-PRB` — probability: conditioning, trees, counting, variance (v6)

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-PRB-01` | Conditioning reversed (P_B(A) given for P_A(B)) | P(sick \| +) taken equal to P(+ \| sick) | P(A) = 0.3, P_A(B) = 0.6, P(B) = 0.5: P_B(A)? **[0.36]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MPI |
| `CB-PRB-02` | Intersection and conditional probability confused | P(A ∩ B) = P_A(B) = 0.5 → 0.2 | P(A) = 0.4, P_A(B) = 0.5: P(A ∩ B)? **[0.2]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-PRB-03` | Tree rules swapped (added along a path, multiplied across paths) or a path forgotten | P(B) = 0.4 + 0.5 → 0.4 × 0.5 + 0.6 × 0.25 | P(A) = 0.4, P_A(B) = 0.5, P_Ā(B) = 0.25: P(B)? **[0.35]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-PRB-04` | Wrong denominator: whole population instead of the conditioning group (base rate) | 150/1 000 → 150/200 | 1 000 tested, 200 positive, 150 sick among them: P(sick \| +)? **[0.75]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → Tle |
| `CB-PRB-05` | Equiprobability assumed without grounds (sums of dice, two colours, numbers of successes) | P(sum = 7) = 1/11 → 1/6 | Two fair dice: P(sum = 2)? **[1/36]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | BR → MP2I |
| `CB-PRB-06` | Independence confused with incompatibility | "independent, so P(A ∩ B) = 0" → P(A)P(B) | A, B independent, P(A) = 0.5, P(B) = 0.4: P(A ∩ B)? **[0.2]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | 1re → MP2I |
| `CB-PRB-07` | Complement taken on the wrong event, or P(at least one) = np | 3 trials, p = 0.2: P(at least one) = 0.6 → 0.488 | Same question **[1 − 0.8³ = 0.488]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MP2I |
| `CB-PRB-08` | Binomial coefficient omitted (one ordering counted) | B(3 ; 0.5): P(X = 1) = 1/8 → 3/8 | B(3 ; 0.4): P(X = 2)? **[0.288]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MP2I |
| `CB-PRB-09` | Variance rules misused: V(aX) = aV(X), V(X + Y) = V(X) + V(Y) without independence, σ added, n forgotten | V(2X) = 2V(X) → 4V(X) | X, Y independent, V(X) = 2, V(Y) = 3: V(2X − Y)? **[11]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MPI |

#### `CB-LIM` — limits, indeterminate forms, equivalents (v6)

| Code | Error (observable) | Wrong → correct | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Type | Evidence | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CB-LIM-01` | Indeterminate form "resolved" by a rule (∞/∞ = 1, 0/0 = 0, ∞ − ∞ = 0, 1^∞ = 1), or ∞ substituted as a number | lim (2n² + 1)/(n² − 3) = 1 → 2 | lim (n² + 1)/(2n² − 3) **[1/2]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MP2I |
| `CB-LIM-02` | Dominant terms mishandled (constant terms kept, or one leading term only) | (2n² + 1)/(n² − 3) → −1/3 → 2 | lim_{x→+∞} (3x² − x)/(x + 1) **[+∞]** | PROC | hyp. (v6) | Tle → MP2I |
| `CB-LIM-03` | Equivalents or expansions added, subtracted or composed without care | sin x − x ~ x − x = 0 → −x³/6 | Equivalent of 1 − cos x at 0 **[x²/2]** | CONC | hyp. (v6) | MP2I → MPI |

### 2A.3 Reasoning and proof codes (`RP`)

| Code | Error (observable) | Typical production | 1-minute probe **[key]** | Levels |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RP-01` | Proof by appearance or by measurement | « On voit que l'angle est droit », « je mesure 90° » | Léa measures 90° and concludes that the angle is right. Is it a proof? **[No: a measurement is approximate]** | BR → 1re |
| `RP-02` | Theorem used in the wrong direction (direct ↔ converse), or converse assumed true | Pythagoras' theorem cited to prove that a triangle is right | x = 2 ⇒ x² = 4. Is the converse true? **[No: x = −2]** | BR → MP2I |
| `RP-03` | Circular reasoning: what must be proved is used as data | "ABCD is a rectangle, so its diagonals are equal, so ABCD is a rectangle" | Spot the flaw in that proof **[the conclusion is used as a premise]** | BR → MP2I |
| `RP-04` | Hypotheses of a theorem not checked (right angle, alignment and order of points, parallelism, continuity, domain, n ∈ ℕ) | Thales applied without stating the alignment; IVT without continuity | Which condition is missing to apply the converse of Thales? **[points aligned in the same order]** | BR → MPI |
| `RP-05` | Generalisation from examples; numerical tests taken as a proof | "True for n = 1, 2, 3, so true for every n" | Is checking 3 values a proof? **[No]** | BR → MPI |
| `RP-06` | Invalid counterexample (does not satisfy the hypothesis), or none given when refuting | Counterexample to "x² = 4 ⇒ x = 2" chosen as x = 3 | Give a valid counterexample **[x = −2]** | BR → MP2I |
| `RP-07` | Rounded values used to establish an exact equality | "5.83² ≈ 34, so the triangle is right" | Can "4.12² ≈ 17" prove a right angle? **[No]** | BR → Tle |
| `RP-08` | Property not stated, conclusion missing, connectors misused (donc / car / or), steps out of order | Calculation without any justification | Complete « On sait que … Or … Donc … » **[data / property / conclusion]** | BR → Tle |
| `RP-09` | Quantifier order or dependence errors (N depends on n; ∃N before ∀ε; a fixed tolerance) | "Let N = n + 1" | In the definition of a limit, which comes first, ε or N? **[for every ε, there is an N]** | Tle → MPI |
| `RP-10` | Negation errors (∀ and ∃ not exchanged, "and/or", strict/large) | Negation of "∀x, f(x) > 0" written "∀x, f(x) ≤ 0" | Negate "∀x ∈ ℝ, x² > 0" **[∃x ∈ ℝ, x² ≤ 0]** | 1re → MPI |
| `RP-11` | Induction errors (no initialisation, P(n + 1) assumed, heredity proved for one fixed n) | Heredity starts with "P(n + 1) is true" | What does the heredity step prove? **[P(n) ⇒ P(n + 1) for every n ≥ n₀]** | Tle → MP2I |
| `RP-12` | Necessary vs sufficient confused; property vs characterisation | "f′(a) = 0, so f has an extremum at a"; "uₙ → 0, so Σuₙ converges" | Is f′(a) = 0 sufficient for an extremum? **[No: x³ at 0]** | 1re → MPI |
| `RP-13` | Existence and uniqueness confused (one proved, the other claimed) | "IVT, so the equation has exactly one solution" | The IVT shows that x³ + x − 1 = 0 has a solution in [0 ; 1]. Does it show there is only one? **[No: uniqueness needs strict monotonicity]** | Tle → MPI |

Error IDs without a transversal code are domain-specific (for example a misread theorem name): keep them untagged rather than forcing a code.

## 2B. Student error-tracking protocol — v5

### 2B.1 Tagging rule (one record per lost point, or per error with no point lost)

1. **Where:** the micro-skill error ID (`SUBDOMAIN-NN-Ek`). If none matches, use `SUBDOMAIN-NN-E0` with a short description; review the `E0` records each term and turn recurring ones into new hypotheses.
2. **Which mechanism:** 0–2 transversal codes (`CB-…`, `RP-…`), pre-filled from §2A.4.
3. **Type:** `SLIP`, `PROC`, `CONC`, `NOTA`, `READ` or `CHECK` (§2A).
4. **Severity:** `S1` cosmetic (notation; result still right) · `S2` local (wrong value, valid method, carried forward) · `S3` blocking (method or argument invalid).
5. One wrong answer is not evidence of several independent deficits (§13), except in an item built to test a prerequisite.
6. **(v6)** `E4`–`E6` are v6 additions with the same status as `E1`–`E3`; IDs are never renumbered or reused.

### 2B.2 Marking with error carried forward (« on ne pénalise qu'une fois »)

- A basic-calculation error costs the execution point of the step where it occurs.
- Later steps that are correct given the wrong value keep their method and conclusion points.
- Exception: no carry-forward on a part the error made easier (for example a sign error that turns a quadratic equation into a linear one).
- Record the `CB` code even when no point is lost (`S1`): unmarked errors are the ones that become persistent.

### 2B.3 Slip or misconception?

| Signal | Probable type | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Corrected without help when the line is pointed out (« Vérifie la ligne 3 ») | `SLIP` | Log it; no remediation |
| Same code in ≥ 2 different items of the same test | `PROC` or `CONC` | Run the matching probe items (§2B.7) |
| The student states the rule correctly but misapplies it | `PROC` | Guided practice on the procedure |
| The student defends the wrong rule (« on ajoute les dénominateurs ») | `CONC` | Re-teach the meaning; confront with a numerical counterexample |
| (v6) Wrong coded distractor answered « Sûr » | `CONC` likely | Probe, then re-teach the meaning (§2C.6) |
| (v6) Wrong answer « Pas sûr », or « Je ne sais pas » | knowledge gap, not a misconception | Teach, then probe; `NSP` is not an error code |
| (v6) Right answer « Pas sûr » | fragile | One more item before calling the skill acquired |

### 2B.4 Persistence statuses (per student × code)

| Status | Rule (design defaults, to adjust on class data) | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `OBSERVED` | 1 occurrence | Self-check reminder written on the script |
| `RECURRENT` | ≥ 2 occurrences in one test, or in 2 tests within 6 weeks | 5-item probe + 10-minute targeted remediation |
| `PERSISTENT` | Observed in ≥ 3 tests, or still present after one remediation cycle | Re-teach the concept; add the code to the student's card (§2B.8); inform the family or tutor if useful |
| `RESOLVED` | 2 consecutive clean probes after remediation, at least 1 week apart | Spaced re-check after 4–6 weeks |

### 2B.5 Error-log schema (one row per tagged error)

```csv
date,student_id,test_id,item_id,micro_skill_id,error_id,transversal_tags,type,severity,points_lost,observed_answer,self_corrected,status_after,remediation_id,retest_date,retest_result
2026-11-05,S014,DIAG-BR-NC-01,EX4,BR-NC-04,BR-NC-04-E1,CB-PAR-04,PROC,S2,1,"(x-5)(x+5)",N,RECURRENT,REM-PAR-04,2026-11-19,OK
```

- `student_id` is a pseudonym (see §2B.9); `transversal_tags` are separated by `;`.
- `status_after` is recomputed from all rows of the same student × code (§2B.4).
- **(v6)** The draft v1.1 handoff (parametric items) adds a 17th column `variant_seed` once implemented; player 1.1.0 exports the 16 columns above. Paper tests that use the confidence tier may add a last column `confidence` (`S` sure, `U` unsure, `NSP`).

**(v7)** Add an `evidence` field to each row (`SUSPECTED` | `CONFIRMED` | `UNDETERMINED`, §2C.6a); an `UNDETERMINED` row lists its candidate codes in `note`.

### 2B.6 Class indicators (per test and per term)

| Indicator | Definition | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | % of students with ≥ 1 occurrence of a code | Whole-class reminder when above 30 % (design default) |
| Recurrence rate | % of students `RECURRENT` or `PERSISTENT` on a code | Small-group remediation |
| Resolution rate | % of remediated students `RESOLVED` at re-test | Evaluate the remediation itself |
| Family profile | Student × `CB` family heat map | Individual plans |
| Top-5 codes | The 5 most frequent codes in the class | Choose the lesson-starter automatisms |

### 2B.7 Basic-calculation probe test (« Test de calcul de base »)

- **Core:** 22 items, about 17 minutes, no calculator, usable from 3e to MP2I entry. **Extension:** 6 items, 6 minutes, Terminale → MP2I. **Extension B (v6):** 6 items, 6 minutes, Terminale → MPI (families `CB-DER`, `CB-PRB`, `CB-LIM`).
- 1 point per item; record the code of every wrong answer. Per family: 0 error = OK; 1 error = `OBSERVED`; ≥ 2 errors = `RECURRENT` → remediation.
- Re-use the probe after remediation with new numbers (same code, same structure).

**Core (student-facing, French):**

| N° | Question (élève) | Réponse attendue | Code |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | (−3) × (−4) × (−1) = | −12 | `CB-SGN-01` |
| 2 | −5 − (−8) = | 3 | `CB-SGN-02` |
| 3 | Développer −(4 − 2x) | −4 + 2x | `CB-SGN-03` |
| 4 | Calculer −2², puis (−2)² | −4, puis 4 | `CB-SGN-04` |
| 5 | Si −x > 0, quel est le signe de x ? | x est négatif | `CB-SGN-08` |
| 6 | 7 + 3 × 5 = | 22 | `CB-PAR-01` |
| 7 | Développer 4(x − 3) | 4x − 12 | `CB-PAR-02` |
| 8 | Calculer x² − 3x pour x = −2 | 10 | `CB-PAR-03` |
| 9 | 1/4 + 2/3 = | 11/12 | `CB-FRA-01` |
| 10 | (3/4) ÷ (9/8) = | 2/3 | `CB-FRA-03` |
| 11 | Simplifier (3x + 6)/3 | x + 2 | `CB-FRA-05` |
| 12 | 10⁵ × 10⁻² = | 10³ | `CB-PUI-01` |
| 13 | 2⁻² = | 1/4 (ou 0,25) | `CB-PUI-03` |
| 14 | √(36 + 64) = | 10 | `CB-PUI-04` |
| 15 | Réduire 5x + 2 − 3x | 2x + 2 | `CB-LIT-01` |
| 16 | Développer (x − 4)² | x² − 8x + 16 | `CB-LIT-03` |
| 17 | Résoudre x² = 25 | x = 5 ou x = −5 | `CB-EQ-03` |
| 18 | Résoudre −3x > 12 | x < −4 | `CB-SGN-06` |
| 19 | Ranger dans l'ordre croissant : 0,4 ; 0,35 ; 0,125 | 0,125 < 0,35 < 0,4 | `CB-NUM-01` |
| 20 | Un prix augmente de 10 %, puis baisse de 10 %. Évolution globale ? | −1 % | `CB-PCT-01` |
| 21 | Coefficient multiplicateur associé à une hausse de 7 % | 1,07 | `CB-PCT-04` |
| 22 | 2,5 m² = … cm² | 25 000 cm² | `CB-UNI-01` |

**Extension (Terminale → MP2I):**

| N° | Question (élève) | Réponse attendue | Code |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | Pour n > 0, 1/n < 10⁻³ équivaut à … | n > 1 000 | `CB-INQ-01` |
| E2 | \|x − 2\| < 0,5 équivaut à … < x < … | 1,5 < x < 2,5 | `CB-INQ-02` |

| E5 | uₙ = 3n − 1. Exprimer u₍ₙ₊₁₎ | 3n + 2 | `CB-NOT-05` |
| E6 | Résoudre √(x + 6) = x | x = 3 (x = −2 est rejeté) | `CB-EQ-04` |


**Extension B (v6 families, Terminale → MPI):**

| N° | Question (élève) | Réponse attendue | Code |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Dériver x² eˣ | (x² + 2x)eˣ | `CB-DER-01` |
| B2 | Dériver (2x + 1)³ | 6(2x + 1)² | `CB-DER-02` |
