### 2B.8 Student self-check card (« Mes erreurs à surveiller », student-facing, French)

| Mon code | Ce que je fais | Ce que je dois faire | Mon exemple corrigé |
|---|---|---|---|
| ex. `CB-SGN-03` | −(2x − 3) = −2x − 3 | Le « − » change le signe de **chaque** terme | −(2x − 3) = −2x + 3 |
| … | … | … | … |

**Routine de vérification (30 secondes à la fin de chaque exercice) :**

1. Signes : un « − » devant une parenthèse change tous les signes.
2. Parenthèses : je mets des parenthèses quand je remplace x par un nombre négatif.
3. Priorités : × et ÷ avant + et −.
4. Fractions : même dénominateur pour additionner ; je ne simplifie jamais des termes.
5. Unités : une unité à chaque ligne ; m² → cm² : × 10 000.
6. Vraisemblance : ordre de grandeur, signe, probabilité entre 0 et 1.
7. Vérification : je remplace la solution dans l'équation de départ.

### 2B.9 Data protection (RGPD)

- Pseudonymise students in every shared log; keep the name ↔ pseudonym table separately and offline.
- Record only what serves teaching (no health or family information); keep logs for the school year, then delete or anonymise them.
- Show each student their own record and card; share class indicators only in aggregate.

## 2C. Diagnostic precision layer — v6

A v5 error was a label ("add denominators"). A v6 error is a **rule with its signature**: the wrong answer the rule produces on the seed. An item is diagnostically precise when (1) every distractor and every lost rubric point maps to exactly one error ID, (2) no error produces the key on the item's numbers, and (3) a conclusion about a student rests on at least two independent observations.

### 2C.1 Reading a row (v6; grouped by seed in v8)

- **Errors cell (v8):** two groups, **MCQ seed:** then **Open seed:**. Each entry `` `ID` label → signature (`tags`) `` gives the answer the error produces on the seed of its group; an error that leaves a trace on both seeds appears in both groups (*same rule* in the second). Quoted text is the typical wrong statement (conceptual errors, reasoned options). *(v6–v7 wrote one list, with `open:` before an open-seed signature.)*
- **Rule R1 — one seed per signature (v8; Codex AUD-014, Grok R1):** compare a signature only with the seed of its group. CSV `mode` `mcq`: `sig_mcq` against `mcq_seed` and `mcq_key`; `open`: `sig_open` against `open_seed` and `open_target`; `mcq+open`: each part against its own seed. Every review finding records the mode and the seed used; before reporting that a signature "answers another question", check it against the other seed.
- `` *(v8)* `` after an ID marks an MCQ-seed error added in v8 so that the row has three distinct MCQ distractors. `` `OPEN_ONLY` `` in the ID cell: fewer than three distinct MCQ-seed signatures, so the row gives open items only (§2C.2 S6). `` anchor `COPY` (provisional) `` or `` anchor `TOC` (provisional) ``: the row's anchor was read in a copy or a table of contents; check it in the BO before release (§1.2 item 27).
- `` **[same value as the key in another form: …]** `` marks a `form_distinct` error: the option is compared as written, and the stem states the required form (§1.2 item 26).
- `` *(v6)* `` after an ID marks an added mechanism (`E4`–`E6`), created where E1–E3 could not give three distinct MCQ distractors. Existing IDs keep their v5 meaning.
- `` `REASONED` `` in the ID cell: binary or conceptual seed; every option is an answer plus a reason, and the signatures are the wrong reasons.
- `` *(v6: …)* `` in the action cell explains a changed seed (masking, collision, guardrail or eligibility).
- Signatures were obtained by applying each rule; the numeric ones were recomputed by script (§15). They are **not** observed student answers: §2A evidence still decides "documented" vs "hypothesis".
- When the generator changes the numbers, it **recomputes** every signature from its rule; a signature is never copied to new numbers.

### 2C.2 Distractor rules (on the item's own numbers)

| Rule | Requirement | Typical failure |
|---|---|---|
| S1 Computed | Each distractor is the signature of one named rule | "key ± 1" or random values |
| S2 Distinct | Key and distractors differ after normalisation: simplified fractions, decimal ↔ fraction, equivalent intervals, equivalent expressions (compare at 3 values) | 4/52 and 1/13; ]−∞ ; 4] and "x ≤ 4" |
| S3 No masking | No rule gives the key on these numbers | r = 2 in a cylinder: πr²h = 2πrh (`BR-GM-02` v5) |
| S4 Single cause | One wrong value ↔ one error; if two rules give the same value, change the numbers, or code the value with both and say so in `why` | "+a % then −b %": adding the rates and taking the second change on the initial value always coincide (`1C-AU-04`) |
| S5 Plausible | A real student can produce it in the time allowed, in the same format as the key | one irrational option among integers |
| S6 No padding | **(v8)** An MCQ needs three distinct signatures on its own seed: only rows with `allowed_items` = `MCQ+OPEN` give an MCQ; an `OPEN_ONLY` row gives an open item (or a `REASONED` item once its reasons are written into the row). On new numbers, if two rules coincide and fewer than three distinct values remain, change the numbers. *(v6–v7 allowed a `LOCAL` distractor with its `why`.)* | invented distractors coded with an unrelated ID; a `LOCAL` option added only to reach four options |
| S7 Positions | Keys spread over A–D (no position above 40 % of the keys); shuffle in digital delivery | key always A (YAML format §8 migration) |

### 2C.3 Choosing discriminating numbers — trap table

| Trap | Errors it hides | v5 seeds fixed in v6 | Use instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 as base, exponent, ratio or radius (2² = 2 × 2 = 2 + 2; πr²h = 2πrh) | power as a product, area vs lateral area, k² vs 2k | `BR-GM-02` | 3, 5, 7 (but for k = 3, 3k = k²) |
| p = 0.5, symmetric tables, Se + FPR = 1 | success ↔ failure, P_A(B) ↔ P_B(A), np² = np(1 − p), base-rate neglect = reversed conditioning | `1C-PR-04`, `TS-PR-02`, `TC-PR-01`, `1S-PS-02` | p = 0.2, 0.3, 0.4; asymmetric tables |
| f(a) = f′(a), fixed points f(n) = n, symmetric points such as (1 ; 1) | value used as a slope, "output = input", swapped coordinates, projection length = distance | `1S-AN-02`, `BR-AP-01`, `1S-AU-04`, `M1-EU-04` | test every rule on the chosen value |
| E(X) = 0 or V(X) = 1 | E(X)² forgotten, σ given for V | `1S-PS-04` | values with E(X) ≠ 0 and V ≠ 1 |
| mean = median = mode, sorted data | unsorted median, mode given | `BR-DF-01` | unsorted lists with distinct mean, median and mode |
| equal parameters (A = B, slope = intercept, symmetric gradient) | swapped parameters | `M1-PO-04`, `BR-DF-05`, `M1-F2-03` | distinct parameters |
| linear integrand or interval of length 1 | endpoint average = mean, width forgotten = integral | `TC-IN-03`, `TS-AP-03` | non-linear f, b − a ≠ 1 |
| Lorenz curve x² (∫L = G = 1/3) | "∫L given as G" | `TC-IN-04` | L(x) = x³ |
| n × k = C(n ; k) (C(5 ; 2) = 10) | "n × k" counting | `TS-AG-02`, `M1-PR-01` | C(6 ; 2) = 15 |
| r = 2 in sums (u₀ + … + u₃ = 3 × 2³) | term vs sum, arithmetic vs geometric | `1S-AL-04` | r = 4 |
| thresholds where > and ≥ agree; vertex inside the domain | strictness; optimum outside the domain | `1C-SE-04`, `TC-FN-04` | threshold reached exactly; domain excluding the vertex |
| zero or unit coefficients | sign errors, forgotten factors | — | non-zero, ≠ ±1, mixed signs |

### 2C.4 Diagnostic item formats

| Format | Use when | Student sees | Reading rule | Time (add to §2) | YAML (`alphavalon.eval`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SINGLE_KEY` MCQ | procedures with ≥ 3 computable errors | key + 3 signatures | the chosen option gives its code | 1–3 min | `mcq`: `code` + `why` on each wrong option |
| `REASONED` MCQ | yes/no, true/false, "which theorem", conceptual seeds | each option = answer + reason | a right answer with a wrong reason is a wrong option: no lucky guess on binary items | +0.5 min | `mcq`, reasons in the option texts |
| `TWO_TIER` | key concepts where guess, slip and misconception must be told apart | tier 1: answer; tier 2: "because…" (4 reasons) | right/right = acquired; right/wrong = fragile (code of the reason); wrong + matching reason = misconception; wrong + unrelated reason = guess (two-tier tests, R20) | +1 min | two consecutive `mcq` items, tier 2 coded |
| Confidence tier | DIAGNOSTIC on paper | « Sûr » / « Pas sûr » after each MCQ | matrix §2C.6 (certainty-of-response method, R21) | +10 s per item | not in player v1.1: paper, or CSV column `confidence` |
| « Je ne sais pas » | DIAGNOSTIC | option E | logged `NSP`: 0 point, no error code, kept out of the error statistics | 0 | not in player v1.1 (needs an `nsp` flag): paper only |
| `ASN` (always / sometimes / never) | conceptual traps | 3–4 statements to classify | one code per statement | 2–3 min | one `mcq` per statement |
| `FIND_ERROR` | every level; ≥ 1 `FIND_ERROR` or `EXPLAIN_WRONG` per DIAGNOSTIC test (§2C.7) | a worked solution with exactly one planted error (a §2A code or a row error) | locate (1) · name (1) · correct (1) · state the rule or check (1) | 4 (D2) – 8 (D3) min | `open`; rubric codes = the planted code |
| `EXPLAIN_WRONG` | follow-up of a `CONC` candidate | « Léo trouve … Explique son erreur. » | the explanation shows whether the student holds the rule | 3 min | `open` |
| `STAGED_OPEN` | default for open items | part a procedure (D1) → b method or justification (D2) → c transfer or proof (D3), each with its expected intermediate result | the first failing part locates the level; error carried forward (§2B.2) | 8–12 min | `open` with `parts`, rubric codes, `errors` |
| `MATCHED_PAIR` | every target code | the same code in 2 non-adjacent items with different surfaces (format, numbers, context) | `RECURRENT` in one test only when both are missed with that code | 0 (blueprint rule) | two items sharing `code` or `tags` |
| `PARAM` | re-tests, homework variants | the same item with drawn numbers | the error rule becomes a formula (`when: reply == …`); masking is checked on 200 draws | 1–3 min | `param` item (handoff v1.1 draft: check player support) |
| `PROBE` | after an observed code | the code's 1-minute probe (§2A) with new numbers | confirms or clears the code | 1 min | `retest` of the code |

### 2C.5 Open questions that locate the error

1. Write 2–3 staged parts (§2C.4) with the **expected intermediate result** of each part.
2. Each rubric criterion = one observable step + its expected value + the **code that explains its loss**, e.g. « J'ai obtenu 3x = 9 » (1 pt; `CB-SGN-05` if 3x = 5). In YAML the code goes on the criterion (`rubric[].code`), so an unticked criterion logs the error.
3. List 2–4 typical errors with their signature on this item (`errors[]`), taken from the row and recomputed for the item's numbers.
4. Add one check or justification sub-question when the skill has a conceptual error (substitute the solution, test a value, state the hypotheses): it separates `PROC` from `CONC`.
5. Default 4-point rubric by difficulty: D1 — data 1, execution 2, check 1 · D2 — data/domain 1, method 1, execution 1, conclusion/check 1 · D3 proof — hypotheses 1, structure and quantifiers 1, key step 1, conclusion 1.
6. Give a re-test with new numbers and the same codes (`retest`).

### 2C.6 Evidence rules

| Observation | Interpretation | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Right, sure | acquired on this item | — |
| Right, unsure | fragile (possible guess) | one more item before "acquired" |
| Coded distractor, sure | misconception likely (`CONC`) | log the code; probe; re-teach the meaning |
| Coded distractor, unsure | procedural gap or guess | log the code; type to confirm by a probe |
| `NSP` | knowledge not yet built | teach (not re-teach); no error code |
| Same code in ≥ 2 items with different surfaces | `RECURRENT` within the test | targeted remediation (§2A.1) |
| Code in 1 item | `OBSERVED` | self-check reminder; plant it again in the next test |
| Several different codes on one skill | fragile skill, no single misconception | skill-level remediation |
| Right MCQ, wrong reason in the matching open part | partial understanding | log the reason's code |

- A skill status needs ≥ 2 items or ≥ 3 points; otherwise it is "indicatif" (the player marks sections with fewer than 3 items).
- A code counts once per item, as in the player.
- `CONC` requires a sure wrong answer, the same code on two surfaces, or the student defending the rule (§2B.3).

#### 2C.6a Evidence state of each observation (v7)

Each tagged observation receives one evidence state **before** it feeds the longitudinal statuses of §2B.4 (`OBSERVED`, `RECURRENT`, `PERSISTENT`, `RESOLVED`). The two scales are distinct: the evidence state qualifies one observation; the status summarises a code across tests. Only `CONFIRMED` evidence supports a misconception diagnosis.

| Evidence state | When | Codes logged | Use in §2B.4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SUSPECTED` | a wrong MCQ option whose signature belongs to one error only | that error ID and its 0–2 transversal codes | counts as one occurrence; never a diagnosis on its own |
| `CONFIRMED` | `SUSPECTED`, plus one of: the matching reason in a `REASONED` or two-tier item; the same code in a second item with a different surface; the mechanism visible in the written work; the student's explanation | the error ID and its codes | counts; `CONC` allowed (§2B.3) |
| `UNDETERMINED` | a lost rubric point with no visible mechanism; a value shared by several errors (`ambiguous_with`); a wrong answer that matches no signature | candidate codes only, in `note` | does not count; plan an item that separates the candidates |

- A shared value never selects one error: log every candidate as `UNDETERMINED`. *(The v7 example, `BR-AP-01-E1` and `E3`, is merged in v8: no declared pair remains, but the rule still applies when new numbers make two rules coincide.)*
- An open answer is `CONFIRMED` only when the step that carries the code is written; a wrong final value alone is `UNDETERMINED`.
- `NSP` is not an error: no code, no evidence state.
- Report the count per state in T8: a skill gap may rest on `SUSPECTED` evidence, a misconception may not.

### 2C.7 Planning a test for diagnosis (target codes)

1. Choose 4–8 **target codes**: codes given by the user or found `RECURRENT`/`PERSISTENT` in earlier logs, then DEPP-documented codes of the level (§2A.2), then the row errors of the selected skills.
2. Plant each target code in ≥ 2 items with different surfaces (`MATCHED_PAIR`), e.g. an MCQ distractor and a rubric criterion.
3. Report **code coverage** (code × items × format) next to skill coverage.
4. Keep ≥ 1 `FIND_ERROR` or `EXPLAIN_WRONG` item per DIAGNOSTIC test (MPI: ≥ 1 proof and ≥ 1 find-the-error, as before).
5. End with a follow-up set: one probe per observed code (§2A, new numbers).
6. **(v7)** Coverage is computed on the **selected blueprint** (the skills and codes chosen for this test), never on the whole catalogue; list the in-scope rows the blueprint leaves out.

### 2C.8 Mapping to the YAML format (`alphavalon.eval/v1`, `v1.1`)

| Reference element | YAML |
|---|---|
| key / signature | `options[]`: `correct: true`, or `code` + `why` (one French sentence saying what the student did) |
| row error ID and transversal tags | `code: <SUBDOMAIN-NN-Ek>` + `tags: [CB-…]`, or `code: CB-…` when the transversal code is the precise diagnosis |
| rubric criterion | `rubric[]: {text, points, code}` |
| typical errors of an open item | `errors[]: {id, text, tags}` |
| probe / re-test | `retest: {prompt, answer}` |
| error rule as a formula | `param` item, `feedback[].when` (draft v1.1) |
| v6 codes absent from codebook v5.1 (`CB-DER-*`, `CB-PRB-*`, `CB-LIM-*`, `RP-13`) | declare them in the quiz `spec.codes` (label, family, `status: proposed`, retest) until codebook v5.2 |
| `allowed_items` = `OPEN_ONLY` (v8) | `type: open` items only from that row |
| `anchor_status` = `PROVISIONAL` (v8) | keep `metadata.status: draft` or `review` until the anchor is checked in the BO; never `validated` |
| eligibility `IN-PROG-2021`, `IN-PROG-2026` (v8) | the format document lists `IN-PROG`, `IN-PROG-2019`, `PREREQUISITE`, `SOURCE-MAPPED`, `BRIDGE`, `HOLD`: write `IN-PROG` and the programme year in `spec.programme.edition`, unless the player's schema accepts the reference value |
| micro-skill error codes absent from the codebook (v8) | declare them in `spec.codes` with the row label and `status: hypothesis` |
| `NSP`, confidence tier | not supported by player v1.1: paper or Markdown output only |

The player checks the code shape `CB-<FAMILY>-NN`; if your copy whitelists families, add `DER`, `PRB` and `LIM`.

## 3. Detailed subdomain and micro-skill reference

**How to read a row (v8):** the errors cell has two groups, **MCQ seed:** (signatures on the MCQ seed) and **Open seed:** (signatures on the open seed); compare each signature only with its own seed (rule R1, §2C.1). `` *(v6)* `` and `` *(v8)* `` mark added errors; `` `REASONED` `` marks a binary or conceptual seed written as answer + reason options; `` `OPEN_ONLY` `` marks a row without an MCQ; `` anchor `COPY`/`TOC` (provisional) `` marks an anchor to check in the BO; `` *(v6: …)* ``, `` *(v7: …)* `` and `` *(v8: …)* `` explain changes. Details in §2C.1.

## 4. Brevet — Cycle 4 / Troisième (`BREVET`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO 2026: cycle 4 transition](https://eduscol.education.gouv.fr/5736/ressources-d-accompagnement-du-programme-de-mathematiques-au-cycle-4?menu_id=361). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `BR-NC` — Numbers & calculation

- **Original coverage:** Signed decimals, fractions, powers (powers of 10, scientific notation), square roots, multiples/divisors, prime factorisation; literal calculation: expand, factorise (incl. a²−b²), 1st-degree equations and inequations, zero-product equations.
- **Original expected capacities:** Calculate fluently with and without a calculator; choose a suitable form of a number; use literal calculation to prove a general property; set up and solve an equation from a problem.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No general 2nd-degree solving (only x² = a and zero-product); no algebraic rules on square roots required; no formal number sets.
- **Original source example:** « Factoriser 9x² − 25. » → (3x − 5)(3x + 5)
- **Suggested prerequisites:** primary fractions and proportionality
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`BR-NC-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `BR.NC.2` | calculate with signed fractions and decimals | Compute -3/4 + 1/2 **[key seed: -1/4]** | Show a common-denominator method for -3/4 + 1/2 **[target: -1/4; sign, denominator, simplification]** | **MCQ seed:** `BR-NC-01-E1` add denominators → −1/3 (`CB-FRA-01`); `BR-NC-01-E2` discard negative sign → 5/4 (`CB-SGN-07`); `BR-NC-01-E3` add absolute values → −5/4 (`CB-SGN-07`) |
| **`BR-NC-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `BR.NC.4` | use powers, scientific notation and roots *(v7: `BR-NC-02-E2` has the value of the key in another form; options are compared as written, not by value; v8: the stem states the required form)* | Write 0.00042 in scientific notation a × 10ⁿ with 1 ≤ a < 10 **[key seed: 4.2×10^-4]** | Compare 4.2×10^-4 and 3.8×10^-3; justify using powers of ten **[target: 4.2×10⁻⁴ < 3.8×10⁻³ because 10⁻⁴ < 10⁻³ (0.00042 < 0.0038); comparing 4.2 and 3.8 alone is the error]** | **MCQ seed:** `BR-NC-02-E1` wrong exponent sign → 4.2×10⁴ (`CB-PUI-06`); `BR-NC-02-E2` coefficient outside [1,10) → 42×10⁻⁵ (equal value, not scientific form) (`CB-PUI-06`) **[same value as the key in another form: the stem asks for that form]**; `BR-NC-02-E3` confuse order of magnitude → 4.2×10⁻³ (places counted from the first zero) (`CB-NUM-05`) <br>**Open seed:** `BR-NC-02-E3` (same rule) → "4.2×10⁻⁴ > 3.8×10⁻³ since 4.2 > 3.8" |

**Open-question rubric template (4 points):** identify data/domain and conditions (1); select and justify method (1); carry out mathematically valid operations (1); state and verify or interpret final answer (1). For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

### `BR-DF` — Data, statistics, probability & functions

- **Original coverage:** Mean, median, range; frequency and equiprobable probability; proportionality, percentages; functions: image, antecedent, f(x) notation, linear and affine functions, graph/table reading.
- **Original expected capacities:** Read and interpret tables and graphs; compute a probability in a simple random experiment; model a situation with a linear or affine function.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No conditional probability, no variance/standard deviation; formal study limited to linear and affine functions (others only through graphs or tables).
- **Original source example:** « f(x) = 3x − 2. Antécédent de 7 ? » → 3
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `BR-GM` — Quantities & measures

- **Original coverage:** Lengths, areas, volumes (prism, cylinder, pyramid, cone, sphere); unit conversions; compound quantities (speed, density, kWh); scale factors k, k², k³.
- **Original expected capacities:** Compute and convert quantities; use area/volume formulas; reason on the effect of an enlargement or reduction.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No calculus-based measure; formulas outside the usual solids are given in the subject.
- **Original source example:** « Un agrandissement de rapport 2 multiplie le volume par ? » → 8
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `BR-EG` — Space & geometry

- **Original coverage:** Pythagoras and converse, Thales and converse, right-triangle trigonometry (cos, sin, tan); transformations (axial/central symmetry, translation, rotation, homothety); 3D sections; latitude/longitude.
- **Original expected capacities:** Write a short deductive proof; compute lengths and angles; recognise and use a transformation.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Trigonometry only in the right triangle; no vectors, no coordinate equations of lines.
- **Original source example:** « Triangle rectangle de côtés de l'angle droit 6 et 8 : hypoténuse ? » → 10
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC, BR-GM
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception (v5 — edition split):** DNB 2027 and 2028 (3e in 2026–27 and 2027–28) follow the **outgoing** cycle 4 programme, which lists « triangles semblables », « cas d'égalité des triangles », « caractérisation angulaire du parallélisme », Pythagore and Thalès « et sa réciproque », and the effect of a translation, symmetry, rotation and homothety on a figure (« les définitions ponctuelles … ne figurent pas au programme »). The **new** cycle 4 programme (BO n°10, 5 March 2026; 5e 2026–27, 4e 2027–28, 3e 2028–29, normally DNB 2029) adds explicit logic on converse and contrapositive (Pythagoras in 4e, Thalès in 3e), the midline theorems (4e) and vectors/translations with Chasles' relation (3e); similar triangles and homotheties were not identified in it in this build [CHECK]. The original exclusion "no vectors" applies to the outgoing edition only.

**Open-question rubric template (4 points):** identify data/domain and conditions (1); select and justify method (1); carry out mathematically valid operations (1); state and verify or interpret final answer (1). For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

#### `BR-EG` v5 extension — geometric demonstrations (« Démontrer »)

- **Scope:** short deductive proofs in plane geometry — data → property (theorem, converse or contrapositive) → conclusion; choosing the direction of a theorem; checking its hypotheses; refuting with a counterexample; chaining two justified steps.
- **Programme anchors:** outgoing programme — « utiliser les notions de géométrie plane pour démontrer »; competence *Raisonner*: « démontrer : utiliser un raisonnement logique et des règles établies ». New programme (BO n°10, 2026) — « démontrer par l'enchainement logique d'arguments et de propriétés »; the contrapositive of a true property is true « sans pour autant que sa réciproque soit vraie »; the angle sum of a triangle must be known « et savoir la démontrer » (5e).
- **Edition tags:** `BOTH` · `OUTGOING` (DNB 2027–2028) · `NEW` (DNB 2029 onwards). Exclude rows whose edition does not match the cohort, unless they are explicitly used as `PREREQUISITE` or enrichment.
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Transversal tags in brackets refer to §2A.
- **Writing template (student-facing, French):** « On sait que … (données). Or … (propriété : si … alors …). Donc … (conclusion). » — converse: « D'après la réciproque du théorème de … » — contrapositive: « Comme … ≠ …, d'après la contraposée du théorème de Pythagore, le triangle n'est pas rectangle. »

**Demonstration rubric (4 points, Brevet):** data and hypotheses stated, including the conditions — right angle, alignment and order of points, parallelism (1) [`RP-04`]; correct property named or stated in the right direction — theorem, converse or contrapositive (1) [`RP-02`]; exact verification — exact values, no rounded equality, calculations shown (1) [`RP-07`]; conclusion that answers the question, with connectors (1) [`RP-08`]. Appearance or measurement used as the argument: no point for the property step [`RP-01`]. **Remediation:** error ID → model proof with the three-line template → proof with a gap to fill → proof to write alone → equivalent re-test.

### `BR-AP` — Algorithms & programming

- **Original coverage:** Block programming (Scratch): sequences, loops, conditions, variables, events; calculation programs.
- **Original expected capacities:** Read, complete or correct a short program; predict its output.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Block language only; no Python required.
- **Original source example:** « Choisir n, ×3, +4. Résultat pour n = −2 ? » → −2
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

## 5. Première — General mathematics specialty (`BAC_1RE_SPE`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO no.14 / 2 Apr 2026 — Première specialty](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/2026/Hebdo14/MENE2602917A). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `1S-AU` — Automatisms

- **Original coverage:** Fractions, powers, percentages and evolutions (multiplier coefficient), literal calculation, graph reading, line equations, proportions.
- **Original expected capacities:** Answer quickly and reliably without a calculator.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Limited to the yearly official automatisms list.
- **Original source example:** « +20 % puis −20 % : évolution globale ? » → −4 %
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC, BR-DF
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `1S-AL` — Algebra

- **Original coverage:** 2nd degree: canonical form, discriminant, factorisation, sign. Sequences: explicit/recursive, arithmetic, geometric, sums 1+…+n and 1+q+…+qⁿ, monotonicity.
- **Original expected capacities:** Solve, factorise and study the sign of a quadratic; model a situation with a sequence.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No formal limit of a sequence (intuitive approach only).
- **Original source example:** « Résoudre x² − 5x + 6 = 0. » → {2 ; 3}
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-AU, BR-DF
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `1S-AN` — Analysis

- **Original coverage:** Derivative as limit of the difference quotient, tangent line, derivative rules (product, quotient, x ↦ g(ax+b)), variations and extrema; exponential (y′ = y, y(0) = 1); unit circle, sine and cosine.
- **Original expected capacities:** Compute derivatives; study variations; use properties of exp; solve simple trigonometric equations.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Limits not formalised; no logarithm (Terminale); no general composite derivative.
- **Original source example:** « Dériver f(x) = x eˣ. » → (x + 1)eˣ
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-AL, 1S-AU
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |

### `1S-GE` — Geometry

- **Original coverage:** Vectors, scalar product (projection, norms, coordinates, Al-Kashi); normal vector, Cartesian line equation, circle equation.
- **Original expected capacities:** Use the scalar product for orthogonality, angles and lengths; find line and circle equations.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Plane geometry only (space in Terminale).
- **Original source example:** « u(2 ; −1), v(1 ; 2) : u·v ? » → 0, vecteurs orthogonaux
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-EG, 1S-AL
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `1S-PS` — Probability & statistics

- **Original coverage:** Conditional probability, trees, total probability formula, independence; discrete random variable, expectation, variance, standard deviation.
- **Original expected capacities:** Model a situation with a tree; compute and interpret E(X).
- **Original stated exclusions:** Finite universes; no binomial law (Terminale).
- **Original source example:** « P(A) = 0,4 ; P_A(B) = 0,5. P(A ∩ B) ? » → 0,2
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-DF, 1S-AU
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** **2026 scope:** first-year finite-universe variables, expectation, variance, simple repeated Bernoulli trials (n ≤ 4). Avoid presenting the general binomial-law chapter as Première compulsory content.

- **Original coverage:** Python: variables, functions, loops, conditions, lists; threshold algorithms, scanning/dichotomy.
- **Original expected capacities:** Write or complete a short Python function.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No advanced data structures.

### `1S-LO` — Set vocabulary & logic

- **Original coverage:** Set notation, implication, equivalence, quantifiers, negation, counterexample, contrapositive, proof by contradiction.
- **Original expected capacities:** Negate a quantified statement; choose an appropriate proof method.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Logic is taught through the content, not as a separate chapter.
- **Original source example:** « Négation de : ∀x ∈ ℝ, x² > 0. » → ∃x ∈ ℝ, x² ≤ 0
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`1S-LO-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · `REASONED` · ref `1S.LO.2` · anchor `COPY` (provisional) | recognise implication and converse | From x=2 follows x²=4; converse? **[key seed: False: x = −2 gives x² = 4 but x ≠ 2]** | Give counterexample to x²=4 ⇒ x=2 **[target: x=-2]** | **MCQ seed:** `1S-LO-01-E1` treat converse automatically valid → "True: the converse of a true statement is true" (`RP-02`); `1S-LO-01-E2` counterexample violates hypothesis → "False: x = 3 gives x² ≠ 4" (`RP-06`); `1S-LO-01-E3` confuse necessary and sufficient → "True: x = 2 is sufficient, so it is necessary" (`RP-12`) |
| **`1S-LO-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `1S.LO.2` · anchor `COPY` (provisional) | negate universal and existential statements | Negation ∀x∈R,x²>0 **[key seed: ∃x∈R,x²≤0]** | Negate ∃x∈R,x>2 and justify **[target: ∀x∈R,x≤2]** | **MCQ seed:** `1S-LO-02-E1` keep same quantifier → ∀x ∈ ℝ, x² ≤ 0 (`RP-10`); `1S-LO-02-E2` negate number not relation → ∀x ∈ ℝ, −x² > 0 (`RP-10`); `1S-LO-02-E3` confuse ≤ with < → ∃x ∈ ℝ, x² < 0 (`RP-10`); `1S-LO-02-E4` *(v6)* only the quantifier changed → ∃x ∈ ℝ, x² > 0 (`RP-10`) |
| **`1S-LO-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `1S.LO.2` · anchor `COPY` (provisional) | use contrapositive and contradiction | Contrapositive P⇒Q **[key seed: ¬Q⇒¬P]** | Prove n² even ⇒ n even by contrapositive **[target: suppose n odd: n = 2k + 1, n² = 2(2k² + 2k) + 1 is odd; this proves "n odd ⇒ n² odd", the contrapositive of "n² even ⇒ n even"]** | **MCQ seed:** `1S-LO-03-E1` prove converse instead → Q ⇒ P (`RP-02`); `1S-LO-03-E4` *(v6)* inverse taken for the contrapositive → ¬P ⇒ ¬Q (`RP-02`); `1S-LO-03-E5` *(v6)* negation taken for the contrapositive → P and ¬Q (`RP-10`) <br>**Open seed:** `1S-LO-03-E2` assume required conclusion → "n² is even, so n = 2k" assumed at the start (`RP-03`); `1S-LO-03-E3` omit integer parity argument → n² = 4k² + 4k + 1 written without concluding it is odd |
| **`1S-LO-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `1S.LO.2` · anchor `COPY` (provisional) | distinguish equality, equivalence and counterexample | x²=1 equivalent to **[key seed: x=±1]** | Solve √(x + 2) = x, analysing each step **[target: conditions x ≥ −2 and x ≥ 0; squaring gives x² − x − 2 = 0, x = 2 or x = −1; x = −1 rejected (x ≥ 0 needed); S = {2}; check √4 = 2]** | **MCQ seed:** `1S-LO-04-E4` *(v6)* one root lost → x = 1 (`CB-EQ-03`); `1S-LO-04-E5` *(v6)* x² read as 2x → x = 1/2 (`CB-LIT-02`); `1S-LO-04-E6` *(v8)* "and" written instead of "or" → "x = 1 and x = −1" <br>**Open seed:** `1S-LO-04-E1` drop domain restriction → no condition x ≥ 0 written (`RP-04`); `1S-LO-04-E2` square and assume equivalence → S = {−1 ; 2} (`CB-EQ-04`); `1S-LO-04-E3` fail verification → x = −1 kept, no substitution check (`CB-VER-02`) |

## 6. Première — Specific mathematics, integrated into scientific studies (`BAC_1RE_COMMUN`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO no.14 / 2 Apr 2026 — Première specific mathematics](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/2026/Hebdo14/MENE2602916A). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `1C-AU` — Automatisms

- **Original coverage:** Simple fractions, powers of 10, decimal/fraction/percentage conversions, mental calculation, orders of magnitude, graph reading (image, antecedent, line equation).
- **Original expected capacities:** Calculate and read graphs without a calculator; check consistency of a result.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Yearly official automatisms list. [TO VERIFY]

### `1C-FN` — Functions

- **Original coverage:** Functions as models: 2nd-degree polynomials, exponential; graphical and local view of the derivative to analyse variations.
- **Original expected capacities:** Use a function to describe a phenomenon; interpret variations.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No formal limits; light formalism. [TO VERIFY]
- **Original source example:** « Lire sur le graphique l'intervalle où f est croissante. »
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1C-AU, BR-DF
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** **(v9: withdrawn — derivation is not in the 2026 programme; `1C-FN-05`…`10` are `HOLD`, §1.2 item 32.)** *(v7 text:)* **(v7, corrects v5–v6)** the 2026 programme (R4, academy copy; also the 2022 text, R31) includes « variation instantanée et globale »: derivative number, tangent and slope, usual derivatives, variations of polynomials of degree ≤ 3. Derivative items are allowed within that scope (`1C-FN-05`…`10`); no formal limits, no quotient or composite derivatives.

### `1C-SE` — Sequences / evolutions

- **Original coverage:** Discrete evolution models (e.g., radioactive decay, half-life) using tables and graphs.
- **Original expected capacities:** Model an evolution step by step; estimate durations or remaining proportions.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No formal sequence theory required. [TO VERIFY]
- **Original source example:** « Une quantité diminue de moitié tous les 5 ans. Reste-t-il après 15 ans ? » → 1/8
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1C-AU, 1C-FN
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** **2026 scope:** arithmetic/geometric sequences and evolution models; no epsilon/limit formalism.

### `1C-SD` — Statistics

- **Original coverage:** Two-variable data, scatter plots, linear and non-linear trends.
- **Original expected capacities:** Read a scatter plot; criticise an extrapolation.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No least-squares formalism required. [TO VERIFY]
- **Original source example:** « Le nuage suggère-t-il une tendance linéaire ? Justifier. »
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1C-AU, BR-DF
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** **2026 scope:** bivariate statistics and affine adjustment, point moyen; least-squares formula derivation is not required.

### `1C-PR` — Probability

- **Original coverage:** Introductory conditional probability: two-way tables, trees (sensitivity, specificity).
- **Original expected capacities:** Read a two-way table; compute a conditional probability.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No random variables formalism. [TO VERIFY]
- **Original source example:** « Sur 200 tests positifs, 150 malades : P(malade | positif) ? » → 0,75
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-DF, 1C-AU
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** **2026 scope:** conditional probability from cross-tables/trees; repeated independent Bernoulli trials are restricted to n ≤ 4 in the 2026 common programme.

| **`1C-PR-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `1C.PR.1` · anchor `COPY` (provisional) | read a two-way table conditionally | 1 000 people: 160 are sick; 200 test positive, of whom 150 are sick. ## 7. Terminale — General mathematics specialty (`BAC_TLE_SPE`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO no.14 / 2 Apr 2026 — Terminale specialty](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/2026/Hebdo14/MENE2602919A). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `TS-AG` — Algebra & geometry

- **Original coverage:** Counting: k-tuples, arrangements, permutations, combinations, Pascal's relation. Space: vectors, lines and planes, orthogonality, distances, parametric representations, Cartesian plane equations.
- **Original expected capacities:** Count with an appropriate model; prove orthogonality; find intersections in space.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No cross product; complex numbers belong to Maths expertes.
- **Original source example:** « Mots de 3 lettres distinctes avec 5 lettres ? » → 60
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-GE, 1S-AL
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

**Open-question rubric template (4 points):** identify data/domain and conditions (1); select and justify method (1); carry out mathematically valid operations (1); state and verify or interpret final answer (1). For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

### `TS-AN` — Analysis

- **Original coverage:** Sequences (induction, limits, comparison, monotone convergence), limits of functions, composition, convexity, continuity and IVT, logarithm, sine/cosine, primitives, differential equations y′ = ay + b and y′ = ay + f, integrals (linearity, integration by parts).
- **Original expected capacities:** Prove by induction; compute limits; study a function fully; compute an integral.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Integrals on a segment only (no improper integrals); no ε-manipulation required.
- **Original source example:** « ∫₀¹ x eˣ dx ? » → 1 (IPP)
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-AN, 1S-AL
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception (v5 — ε and formalism):** the programme in force for Terminale in 2026–27 (BO spécial n°8, 25 July 2019) defines limits with intervals — « tout intervalle ouvert contenant ℓ contient toutes les valeurs uₙ à partir d'un certain rang »; « tout intervalle de la forme [A ; +∞[ » for +∞ — and states « la maîtrise complète du formalisme n'est pas un attendu » and « les symboles ∀ et ∃ ne sont pas exigibles ». Tolerance and threshold work is therefore in programme; **ε–N and ε–δ proofs are not Terminale content** and are tagged `BRIDGE → MP2I` below. The programme for 2027–28 (BO n°14, 2 April 2026) could not be opened in this build: its formalism level is `HOLD` until checked.

**Open-question rubric template (4 points):** identify data/domain and conditions (1); select and justify method (1); carry out mathematically valid operations (1); state and verify or interpret final answer (1). For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

#### `TS-AN` v5 extension — limits with a tolerance (towards ε) and infinitesimal calculus

- **Scope:** (a) limits handled with an explicit tolerance or threshold — ranks, intervals ]ℓ − r ; ℓ + r[, [A ; +∞[, the official proofs on sequences, and a bridge to ε–N / ε–δ; (b) infinitesimal calculus — difference quotient, tangent approximation and convexity, rectangle bracketing of an integral, limits recognised as derivatives.
- **Eligibility tags:** `IN-PROG-2019` = in the 2019 programme (definition or proof quoted from the official PDF) · `PREREQUISITE` = Première content re-tested in Terminale · `SOURCE-MAPPED` = consistent with the programme, wording not verified · `BRIDGE → MP2I` = beyond Terminale: **excluded from `BAC_TLE_SPE` diagnostics by default**; include it only for a Terminale → MP2I entry profile or on explicit request, and count it in the MP2I prerequisite share.
- **Official anchors (2019):** proofs « Toute suite croissante non majorée tend vers +∞ », « Divergence vers +∞ d'une suite minorée par une suite divergeant vers +∞ », limit of (qⁿ) via Bernoulli's inequality, « Si f″ est positive, alors la courbe représentative de f est au-dessus de ses tangentes »; the integral is defined as an area and « mis en regard » with sums Σ f(xᵢ)Δxᵢ; the rectangle method brackets sums; threshold search is an algorithm example.
- **Edition note:** for the 2026–27 Première, academic presentations of the new programme mention « approximation linéaire » (f(a + h) ≈ f(a) + f′(a)h) and the derivative computed from its definition (square, inverse) [CHECK in BO n°14, 2026]. Students in Terminale in 2026–27 did not have that wording; `TS-AN-14` is written with the tangent equation, which both editions contain.

**ε progression ladder (use it to locate where the chain breaks):**

| 2nde (2019 programme) | « Représentation de l'intervalle [a − r , a + r] puis caractérisation par la condition \|x − a\| ⩽ r »; product of an inequality by a negative number | `CB-INQ-02`, `CB-SGN-06` |
| 1re spécialité | Derivative as the limit of the rate of change (intuitive limit); threshold algorithms | `1S-AN-02`, `1S-AP-02`, `TS-AN-13` |
| Tle spécialité (2019) | Interval definitions « à partir d'un certain rang »; explicit rank for a numerical tolerance; official proofs; tangents and convexity; rectangle bracketing | `TS-AN-06`, `TS-AN-07`, `TS-AN-09`, `TS-AN-10`, `TS-AN-11`, `TS-AN-14`, `TS-AN-15`, `TS-AN-16` |
| Bridge Tle → MP2I | ε–N with quantifiers in order; negation of convergence; ε–δ continuity | `TS-AN-08`, `TS-AN-12`, `TS-AN-17` → `M1-LO-01`, `M1-SU-02`, `M1-LC` |
| MP2I | Supremum, ε–N proofs, Bolzano–Weierstrass, ε–δ | `M1-SU`, `M1-LC` |

**Rubric for tolerance / threshold items (4 points):** inequality \|uₙ − ℓ\| < tolerance (or uₙ > A) set up (1) [`CB-INQ-02`]; solved in the right direction (1) [`CB-INQ-01`]; rank N given as an integer with the right strictness, not as a term value (1) [`CB-EQ-07`, `CB-NOT-05`]; "for every n ≥ N" justified and quantifiers in order (1) [`RP-05`, `RP-09`]. **Remediation:** error ID → numerical exploration (table of uₙ) → guided inequality solving → proof for an arbitrary tolerance → equivalent re-test.

### `TS-PR` — Probability

- **Original coverage:** Bernoulli scheme, binomial law; sums of random variables, linearity of expectation, variance of a sum of independent variables; Bienaymé-Tchebychev, concentration, weak law of large numbers.
- **Original expected capacities:** Recognise a binomial law; compute E and V; use concentration inequalities.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No continuous (density) laws in spécialité.
- **Original source example:** « X ~ B(10 ; 0,5) : E(X), V(X) ? » → 5 ; 2,5
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-PS
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

## 8. Terminale — Advanced mathematics option (`BAC_TLE_EXPERTES`)

**Official source gateway:** [2019/2020 Éduscol advanced mathematics option](https://eduscol.education.gouv.fr/sites/default/files/document/spe264annexe1158825pdf-84165.pdf). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `TE-CX` — Complex numbers

- **Original coverage:** Algebraic, trigonometric and exponential forms; conjugate, modulus, argument; Euler and Moivre formulas; nth roots of unity; polynomial equations with real coefficients; geometric applications.
- **Original expected capacities:** Switch between forms; solve equations in ℂ; prove alignment/orthogonality with complex numbers.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No complex analysis; polynomial equations limited to real coefficients.
- **Original source example:** « Module et argument de 1 + i ? » → √2 ; π/4
- **Suggested prerequisites:** TS-AG, 1S-AN
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `TE-AR` — Arithmetic

- **Original coverage:** Divisibility, Euclidean division, congruences, GCD, Bézout, Gauss, primes, Fermat's little theorem; applications (cryptography, check digits).
- **Original expected capacities:** Solve congruences; use Bézout/Gauss in proofs.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No ℤ/nℤ ring structure.
- **Original source example:** « 2¹⁰ mod 11 ? » → 1 (Fermat)
- **Suggested prerequisites:** BR-NC, 1S-LO
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |

### `TE-GM` — Graphs & matrices

- **Original coverage:** Matrix operations, inverse (order 2–3); graphs, adjacency matrix, paths; Markov chains, stable state.
- **Original expected capacities:** Use matrix powers to count paths or compute states.
- **Original stated exclusions:** No eigenvalue theory.
- **Original source example:** « Nombre de chemins de longueur 2 de i à j ? » → coefficient (i, j) de A²
- **Suggested prerequisites:** TS-AG, TS-PR
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

## 9. Terminale — Complementary mathematics option (`BAC_TLE_COMPLEMENTAIRES`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO no.14 / 2 Apr 2026 — complementary maths](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/2026/Hebdo14/MENE2902920A). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `TC-SE` — Sequences & evolution models

- **Original coverage:** Arithmetic and geometric sequences, limits, population and finance models (loans, amortisation).
- **Original expected capacities:** Model a real evolution with a sequence; interpret its limit.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Applied approach; no formal proofs of convergence.
- **Original source example:** « Capital de 1 000 € à 3 %/an : valeur après n ans ? » → 1 000 × 1,03ⁿ
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1C-SE, 1C-AU
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### `TC-FN` — Functions

- **Original coverage:** Derivatives, convexity, exponential and logarithm as models (marginal cost, thresholds).
- **Original expected capacities:** Study variations and convexity in context.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Context-based; no theoretical analysis.
- **Original source example:** « Coût C(q). Interpréter C′(q). » → coût marginal
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-AN (only if actually previously studied), 1C-FN
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| **`TC-BA-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `TC.BA.1` | reverse conditional probability using Bayes | P(disease)=.01, Se=Sp=.99, PPV **[key seed: .5]** | Compute P(disease\|positive) from test characteristics and explain denominator **[target: P(+) = 0.01 × 0.99 + 0.99 × 0.01 = 0.0198; P(D \| +) = 0.0099/0.0198 = 0.5; the denominator counts all positives, true and false]** | **MCQ seed:** `TC-BA-01-E1` confuse sensitivity with PPV → 0.99 (`CB-PRB-01`); `TC-BA-01-E2` ignore false positives → 1 (false positives left out) (`CB-PRB-04`); `TC-BA-01-E3` reverse events → 0.0099 (P(D ∩ +), no division) (`CB-PRB-02`) |
| **`TC-BA-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `TC.BA.1` | represent prevalence with frequency counts | In 10000 people, 1% disease **[key seed: 100]** | Build a 10,000-person contingency table at 1% prevalence, 99% Se/Sp **[target: sick 100 (99 +, 1 −); healthy 9 900 (99 +, 9 801 −); 198 positives, of whom 99 sick: PPV = 0.5]** | **MCQ seed:** `TC-BA-02-E2` mix relative/absolute counts → 1 (1 % read as 1 person) (`CB-PCT-05`); `TC-BA-02-E4` *(v6)* decimal shift → 1 000 (`CB-NUM-02`); `TC-BA-02-E5` *(v6)* healthy count given → 9 900 <br>**Open seed:** `TC-BA-02-E1` apply specificity to diseased → 99 sick people counted as negative; `TC-BA-02-E3` discard rounding basis → rounded counts that no longer add up (`CB-NUM-04`) |
| **`TC-BA-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `TC.BA.1` | assess sensitivity/specificity and false positives | Specificity=90%, false-positive rate **[key seed: 10%]** | Explain tradeoff between false-positive rate and prevalence in PPV **[target: PPV = Se·p/(Se·p + (1 − Sp)(1 − p)); with Se = Sp = 0.9: p = 0.1 gives 0.5, p = 0.01 gives ≈ 0.083: when p falls, false positives dominate]** | **MCQ seed:** `TC-BA-03-E1` equate sensitivity with specificity → 90 %; `TC-BA-03-E3` wrong complement → "the false-negative rate is 10 %"; `TC-BA-03-E4` *(v6)* decimal → 1 % <br>**Open seed:** `TC-BA-03-E2` treat PPV independent of prevalence → "PPV does not depend on the prevalence" |
| **`TC-BA-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · `REASONED` · ref `TC.BA.1` | communicate Bayesian result ethically and accurately | Low prevalence can make PPV lower than Se **[key seed: True: when the disease is rare, false positives can outnumber true positives]** | Explain to patient why 99%-accurate test may yield PPV near 50% at 1% prevalence **[target: in 10 000 people at 1 % prevalence: 99 true and 99 false positives, so a positive result means a 50 % chance of disease; the test is still useful (1 % becomes 50 %) and a confirmation test is needed]** | **MCQ seed:** `TC-BA-04-E1` claim test is unreliable overall → "True: the test is unreliable"; `TC-BA-04-E2` ignore base rates → "False: PPV is always equal to the sensitivity" (`CB-PRB-01`); `TC-BA-04-E3` assert definitive diagnosis from screening → "False: a positive screening test confirms the disease" |

**Open-question rubric template (4 points):** identify data/domain and conditions (1); select and justify method (1); carry out mathematically valid operations (1); state and verify or interpret final answer (1). For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

### `TC-LO` — Set vocabulary & logic (v7)

- **Official anchor:** `TC.LO.1` — implication, converse, negation, counterexample (∀ and ∃ not exigible), R28.
- **Eligibility:** `IN-PROG-2019` (text in force in 2026–27); new subdomain in v7.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-LO-01` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · `REASONED` · ref `TC.LO.1` | state the converse of an implication and test it | "If a whole number ends in 0, it is divisible by 5." Its converse, and is it true? **[key seed: "if it is divisible by 5, it ends in 0: false"]** | Give a counterexample to the converse **[target: 15]** | **MCQ seed:** `TC-LO-01-E1` inverse given as the converse → "if it does not end in 0, it is not divisible by 5: false"; `TC-LO-01-E2` contrapositive given as the converse → "if it is not divisible by 5, it does not end in 0: true"; `TC-LO-01-E3` converse taken to be true → "if it is divisible by 5, it ends in 0: true" (`RP-02`) |

## 10. CPGE MP2I — Year 1 (`MP2I`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO spécial no.1 / 11 Feb 2021 — MP2I](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/21/Special1/ESRS2035779A.htm). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `M1-LO` — Reasoning & set vocabulary

- **Original coverage:** Logic, quantifiers, sets, maps (injection, surjection, bijection), relations (equivalence, order).
- **Original expected capacities:** Write and negate quantified statements; structure a proof.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Formal logic and set theory beyond the basics are out of program.
- **Original source example:** « Nier : ∀ε > 0, ∃N, ∀n ≥ N, |uₙ − ℓ| ≤ ε. »
- **Suggested prerequisites:** TS-LO, 1S-LO
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M1-LO-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.01.1` | negate nested quantified statements correctly | Negate ∀x∈R ∃y∈R, y>x **[key seed: ∃x ∀y, y≤x]** | Negate ε-N convergence statement including variable ranges and order **[target: ∃ε > 0, ∀N ∈ ℕ, ∃n ≥ N, \|uₙ − ℓ\| > ε]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-01-E1` preserve quantifiers → ∀x ∃y, y ≤ x (`RP-10`); `M1-LO-01-E2` negate only final inequality → same production as E1 (∀x ∃y, y ≤ x): code E1 (`RP-10`); `M1-LO-01-E3` swap ∀ and ∃ order → ∀y ∃x, y ≤ x (`RP-10`); `M1-LO-01-E4` *(v6)* quantifiers switched, inequality not negated → ∃x ∀y, y > x (`RP-10`) |
| **`M1-LO-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MP2I.01.3` | analyse injectivity, surjectivity and bijectivity | x↦x² on R is injective? **[key seed: No: (−1)² = 1², so two different numbers have the same image]** | Prove f:R→R,x↦2x+3 is bijective and find inverse **[target: 2x + 3 = y ⇔ x = (y − 3)/2: exactly one antecedent for every y ∈ ℝ, so f is bijective and f⁻¹(y) = (y − 3)/2]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-02-E2` conflate injective/surjective → "No, because −1 has no antecedent" (non-surjectivity given as the reason); `M1-LO-02-E3` assume monotone globally without check → "Yes, because it is increasing"; `M1-LO-02-E4` *(v8)* x² = y² taken to give x = y (negative root forgotten) → "Yes: x² = y² implies x = y" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-LO-02-E1` ignore codomain → "x ↦ x² is a surjection of ℝ onto ℝ" (image [0 ; +∞[ ignored) |
| **`M1-LO-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.01.4` | use equivalence/order relations | Congruence mod 3 is **[key seed: equivalence relation]** | On ℝ, x R y ⇔ \|x − y\| ≤ 1: reflexive, symmetric, transitive? Prove or give a counterexample **[target: reflexive (\|x − x\| = 0 ≤ 1), symmetric; not transitive: 0 R 1 and 1 R 2 but not 0 R 2]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-03-E1` confuse symmetric and antisymmetric → "an order relation"; `M1-LO-03-E4` *(v6)* transitivity denied → "not transitive"; `M1-LO-03-E5` *(v8)* classes counted as for parity → "an equivalence relation with 2 classes" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-LO-03-E2` forget reflexivity → reflexivity not checked; `M1-LO-03-E3` generalise from examples → transitivity "checked" on 0, 0.5, 1 (`RP-05`) |
| **`M1-LO-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `MP2I.01.1` | structure proof using implication or contradiction | To refute ∀x P(x) need **[key seed: one counterexample]** | Prove that the neutral element of a group is unique **[target: if e and e′ are neutral, e = e e′ (e′ neutral) = e′ (e neutral)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-04-E3` confuse example with proof → "check P on many values" (examples instead of one counterexample) (`RP-05`); `M1-LO-04-E4` *(v6)* negation too strong → "a proof that ¬P(x) for every x" (`RP-10`); `M1-LO-04-E5` *(v6)* counterexample that satisfies P → "an x with P(x) true" (`RP-06`) <br>**Open seed:** `M1-LO-04-E1` circular argument → "e = e′ because the neutral element is unique" (`RP-03`); `M1-LO-04-E2` show only existence → existence of a neutral element recalled, uniqueness not addressed (`RP-13`) |
| **`M1-LO-05` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.01.3` | compute a preimage under a map | f: ℝ → ℝ, x ↦ x². Preimage f⁻¹([1 ; 4])? **[key seed: "[−2 ; −1] ∪ [1 ; 2]"]** | Is f: ℝ → [0 ; +∞[, x ↦ x² surjective? injective? **[target: surjective, not injective]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-05-E1` negative antecedents forgotten → "[1 ; 2]" (`CB-EQ-03`); `M1-LO-05-E2` direct image computed → "[1 ; 16]"; `M1-LO-05-E3` f⁻¹ read as 1/f → "[1/4 ; 1]" |
| **`M1-LO-06` · D2** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MP2I.01.3` | deduce injectivity from a composite | f: E → F and g: F → G with g∘f injective. Then **[key seed: "f is injective: f(x) = f(y) ⇒ g(f(x)) = g(f(y)) ⇒ x = y"]** | Give f and g with g∘f injective and g not injective **[target: f: [0 ; +∞[ → ℝ, x ↦ x and g: x ↦ x²]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-06-E1` the map applied last taken as injective → "g is injective, because g is applied last"; `M1-LO-06-E2` property spread to both maps → "f and g are injective, because their composite is"; `M1-LO-06-E3` injective and surjective cases mixed → "f is surjective, by analogy with g∘f surjective ⇒ g surjective" |
| **`M1-LO-07` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.01.1` | set up a two-step induction | u₀ = 2, u₁ = 3, u(n+2) = 3u(n+1) − 2uₙ. To prove uₙ = 2ⁿ + 1 by two-step induction, the initialisation checks **[key seed: "n = 0 and n = 1"]** | Heredity: express u(n+2) from the two previous terms **[target: 2ⁿ⁺² + 1 (heredity step from the recurrence)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LO-07-E1` one initial value only → "n = 0 only" (`RP-11`); `M1-LO-07-E2` initial ranks shifted → "n = 1 and n = 2"; `M1-LO-07-E3` a computed term used as initialisation → "n = 2, computed from the recurrence" |

- **Original expected capacities:** Manipulate sums and indices fluently.
- **Original stated exclusions:** —
- **Original source example:** « Σₖ₌₀ⁿ C(n,k) ? » → 2ⁿ
- **Suggested prerequisites:** 1S-AL, TS-AN
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| **`M1-CA-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.02.1` | handle double sums and order of summation | Σ(i=1..2)Σ(j=1..3)1 **[key seed: 6]** | Evaluate Σ(i=1..n)Σ(j=1..i)1 **[target: n(n+1)/2]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-CA-03-E3` collapse independent indices → 5 (bounds added); `M1-CA-03-E4` *(v6)* outer sum only → 2; `M1-CA-03-E5` *(v8)* inner sum only → 3 <br>**Open seed:** `M1-CA-03-E1` assume rectangular domain → n² (`CB-NOT-05`); `M1-CA-03-E2` reverse limits incorrectly → Σ_{j=1}^{n} Σ_{i=1}^{j} written after the interchange (should be Σ_{j=1}^{n} Σ_{i=j}^{n}) |

### `M1-TA` — Calculus techniques

- **Original coverage:** Usual functions, derivation, primitives; linear differential equations of order 1 and 2 (constant coefficients).
- **Original expected capacities:** Solve standard ODEs; compute primitives.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Proof of the order-2 existence result is out of program.
- **Original source example:** « Résoudre y″ + y = 0. » → y = A cos x + B sin x
- **Suggested prerequisites:** TS-AN
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M1-TA-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.04.6` | differentiate and integrate usual elementary functions | Primitive of 1/x for x>0 **[key seed: ln x+C]** | Find primitive of 2x e^(x²) **[target: e^(x²)+C]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-01-E1` forget additive constant → ln x (one primitive given for all of them) (`CB-DER-05`); `M1-TA-01-E3` use absolute log domain incorrectly → "ln\|x\| + C on ℝ" (`RP-04`); `M1-TA-01-E4` *(v6)* derivative given → −1/x² + C (`CB-DER-03`) <br>**Open seed:** `M1-TA-01-E2` omit chain derivative → x² e^(x²) (`CB-DER-02`) |
| **`M1-TA-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.04.7` | solve first-order linear ODE | y'=2y,y(0)=1 **[key seed: e^(2x)]** | Solve y'+2y=4 with y(0)=0 **[target: y=2−2e^(−2x)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-02-E2` wrong characteristic exponent → e⁻²ˣ; `M1-TA-02-E3` initial condition omitted → Ce²ˣ (`CB-DER-05`); `M1-TA-02-E4` *(v6)* roles of the coefficients swapped → 2eˣ <br>**Open seed:** `M1-TA-02-E1` homogeneous-only solution → y = 0 (homogeneous solution only) (`CB-DER-05`) |
| **`M1-TA-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.04.8` | solve constant-coefficient second-order ODE | y''+y=0 **[key seed: A cos x+B sin x]** | Solve y''−3y'+2y=0 with initial conditions y(0)=1,y'(0)=0 **[target: 2e^x−e^(2x)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-03-E1` wrong characteristic roots → Aeˣ + Be⁻ˣ; `M1-TA-03-E2` count one free constant → A cos x; `M1-TA-03-E4` *(v6)* complex solution given → Ae^(ix) <br>**Open seed:** `M1-TA-03-E3` mismatch derivative initial value → y′(0) = 0 written A + B = 0 (inconsistent with A + B = 1) |
| **`M1-TA-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `MP2I.04.2` | use elementary differentiation rules with parameters | d/dx e^(3x) **[key seed: 3e^(3x)]** | Differentiate ln(2x+1) on x>−1/2 and state domain **[target: x ↦ ln(2x + 1) is defined and differentiable on ]−1/2 ; +∞[, derivative 2/(2x + 1)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-04-E1` omit chain multiplier → e³ˣ (`CB-DER-02`); `M1-TA-04-E3` confuse derivative with inverse → ln(3x); `M1-TA-04-E4` *(v6)* power rule used on the exponent → 3x e³ˣ⁻¹ <br>**Open seed:** `M1-TA-04-E2` neglect logarithm domain → domain ℝ (`RP-04`) |
| **`M1-TA-05` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.04.4` | differentiate a composite with arctan | (arctan(2x))′ **[key seed: 2/(1 + 4x²)]** | arctan x + arctan(1/x) for x > 0 **[target: π/2]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-05-E1` inner derivative forgotten → 1/(1 + 4x²) (`CB-DER-02`); `M1-TA-05-E2` (2x)² taken as 2x² → 2/(1 + 2x²) (`CB-PAR-04`); `M1-TA-05-E3` arctan′ taken as 1/(1 − x²) → 2/(1 − 4x²) |
| **`M1-TA-06` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.04.4` | differentiate hyperbolic functions | ((sh x)′ ; (ch x)′) **[key seed: (ch(x) ; sh(x))]** | Solve ch x = 2 **[target: {−ln(2 + √3) ; ln(2 + √3)}]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-06-E1` signs copied from cos and sin → (ch(x) ; −sh(x)); `M1-TA-06-E2` each function taken as its own derivative → (sh(x) ; ch(x)); `M1-TA-06-E3` both signs changed → (−ch(x) ; sh(x)) |
| **`M1-TA-07` · D2** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.04.6` | integrate with the substitution u = 1 + x² | ∫ from 0 to 1 of x/(1 + x²) dx **[key seed: ln(2)/2]** | ∫ from 0 to π/2 of sin x cos²x dx with u = cos x **[target: 1/3]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-TA-07-E1` factor 1/2 of du = 2x dx lost → ln(2); `M1-TA-07-E2` arctan pattern used → π/4; `M1-TA-07-E3` integral of a product taken as a product of integrals → π/8 (`CB-DER-01`) |

### `M1-SU` — Real numbers & sequences

- **Original coverage:** Supremum property, sequences, limits (ε-N), subsequences, Bolzano-Weierstrass, adjacent and recursive sequences.
- **Original expected capacities:** Prove convergence rigorously with quantifiers.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Constructions of ℝ are out of program.
- **Original source example:** « Montrer que uₙ = 1/n tend vers 0 (ε-N). »
- **Suggested prerequisites:** TS-AN, M1-LO
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Bridge link (v5):** Terminale bridge rows `TS-AN-08`, `TS-AN-12` and `TS-AN-17` check ε readiness before `M1-SU-02` and `M1-LC`; in MP2I diagnostics tag them `PREREQUISITE`. Transversal codes to watch: `CB-INQ-01`, `CB-INQ-02`, `RP-09`, `RP-10`.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M1-SU-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.05.2` | use supremum/infimum correctly | sup(0,1) **[key seed: 1, not attained]** | Prove sup{1−1/n:n≥1}=1 and explain no maximum **[target: 1 bounds the set above (1 − 1/n < 1); for ε > 0, 1 − 1/n > 1 − ε when n > 1/ε, so no smaller upper bound: sup = 1; 1 − 1/n = 1 has no solution, so there is no maximum]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-01-E1` confuse sup with max → "max = 1"; `M1-SU-01-E4` *(v6)* open interval taken as having no supremum → "no supremum"; `M1-SU-01-E5` *(v8)* supremum taken as a largest decimal below 1 → "0.99…, the largest element" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-SU-01-E2` do not establish upper bound → approximation property shown, upper bound not; `M1-SU-01-E3` ignore sequence indexing → set written from n = 0 (1 − 1/0) (`CB-NOT-05`) |
| **`M1-SU-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.05.3` | write rigorous ε-N limit proof *(v6: MCQ seed made quantitative (the limit 0 alone could not reveal E1–E3))* | uₙ = 1/n and ε = 10⁻³: which N gives \|uₙ\| < ε for every n ≥ N? **[key seed: N = 1001]** | Prove ∀ε>0 ∃N ∀n≥N: \|1/n\|<ε and choose N>1/ε **[target: fix ε > 0; N = ⌊1/ε⌋ + 1 > 1/ε; for n ≥ N, 0 < 1/n ≤ 1/N < ε]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-02-E1` choose N depending on n → "N = n + 1" (`RP-09`); `M1-SU-02-E4` *(v6)* strict inequality lost → N = 1000 (`CB-EQ-07`); `M1-SU-02-E5` *(v8)* ε given as the rank → "N = 0.001" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-SU-02-E2` wrong quantifier order → N chosen first, ε afterwards (`RP-09`); `M1-SU-02-E3` infer convergence only numerically → values of 1/n listed for n = 10, 100, 1000 (`RP-05`) |
| **`M1-SU-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.05.4` | analyse monotone bounded or adjacent sequences | Increasing and upper-bounded sequence **[key seed: convergent]** | Show monotone convergence of recurrence u(n+1)=sqrt(2+u_n) for suitable initial u0 **[target: u₀ = 0; by induction 0 ≤ uₙ ≤ uₙ₊₁ ≤ 2 (x ↦ √(2 + x) increasing, √(2 + 2) = 2); increasing and bounded above, so it converges to ℓ ∈ [0 ; 2]; by continuity ℓ = √(2 + ℓ), so ℓ = 2]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-03-E4` *(v6)* limit taken equal to the bound → "converges to its upper bound M" (`RP-12`); `M1-SU-03-E5` *(v8)* upper bound ignored → "(uₙ) tends to +∞"; `M1-SU-03-E6` *(v8)* boundedness taken as the only conclusion → "(uₙ) is bounded, nothing more" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-SU-03-E1` assert boundedness without proof → "uₙ ≤ 2" stated without proof (`RP-04`); `M1-SU-03-E2` confuse fixed point with convergence proof → "ℓ = √(2 + ℓ) gives ℓ = 2, so uₙ → 2" (convergence not proved) (`RP-12`); `M1-SU-03-E3` check only first few terms → convergence inferred from u₀ … u₄ (`RP-05`) |
| **`M1-SU-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `MP2I.05.5` | use subsequences and Bolzano-Weierstrass | Every bounded real sequence has **[key seed: convergent subsequence]** | Find two subsequences of (-1)^n and show whole sequence diverges **[target: u₂ₙ = 1 → 1 and u₂ₙ₊₁ = −1 → −1: two subsequences with different limits, so (uₙ) has no limit]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-04-E1` mistake bounded implies convergent → "a limit" (bounded, so convergent) (`RP-12`); `M1-SU-04-E4` *(v6)* uniqueness assumed → "a unique accumulation point"; `M1-SU-04-E5` *(v8)* supremum taken as a limit point → "a subsequence tending to its supremum" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-SU-04-E2` choose subsequences with same limit incorrectly → two even subsequences chosen; `M1-SU-04-E3` ignore parity → parity of the indices not used |
| **`M1-SU-05` · D2** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MP2I.05.4` | recognise adjacent sequences | uₙ = 1 − 1/n and vₙ = 1 + 1/n² (n ≥ 1). Are they adjacent? **[key seed: "yes: (uₙ) increases, (vₙ) decreases and vₙ − uₙ → 0"]** | Show that uₙ = Σ_{k=1}^{n} 1/k² and wₙ = uₙ + 1/n are adjacent **[target: u increases, w(n+1) − w(n) = 1/(n + 1)² − 1/(n(n + 1)) < 0, w − u → 0]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-05-E1` same limit taken as sufficient → "yes, because both tend to 1" (`RP-12`); `M1-SU-05-E2` difference required to vanish → "no, because vₙ − uₙ is never 0"; `M1-SU-05-E3` one condition only → "yes, because uₙ ≤ vₙ for every n" (`RP-12`) |
| **`M1-SU-06` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.05.8` | solve a linear recurrence of order 2 | General term of u(n+2) = 5u(n+1) − 6uₙ **[key seed: A·2ⁿ + B·3ⁿ]** | u₀ = 0, u₁ = 1: explicit uₙ **[target: 3ⁿ − 2ⁿ]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-06-E1` characteristic roots with the wrong sign → A·(−2)ⁿ + B·(−3)ⁿ (`CB-SGN-09`); `M1-SU-06-E2` characteristic equation written r² + 5r − 6 = 0 → A + B·(−6)ⁿ (`CB-SGN-05`); `M1-SU-06-E3` coefficients taken as the roots → A·5ⁿ + B·6ⁿ |
| **`M1-SU-07` · D2** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.05.2` | find the supremum of a set defined by a sequence | A = {(−1)ⁿ + 1/n : n ≥ 1}. sup A? **[key seed: 3/2]** | inf A; is it attained? **[target: −1 (not attained)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-SU-07-E1` limit of the even terms given → 1; `M1-SU-07-E2` first term given → 0; `M1-SU-07-E3` sum of the suprema of the two parts → 2 |
| **`M1-SU-08` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.05.9` | study u(n+1) = f(uₙ) with the fixed points | u(n+1) = uₙ², u₀ = 1/2. ### `M1-LC` — Limits, continuity, differentiability, convexity

- **Original coverage:** IVT, extreme value theorem, Rolle, mean value theorem, Cᵏ functions, convexity and Jensen.
- **Original expected capacities:** Apply the big theorems with checked hypotheses.
- **Original stated exclusions:** —
- **Original source example:** « f continue sur [a ; b] ⇒ f bornée et atteint ses bornes. »
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-TA, M1-SU
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M1-LC-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.06.3` | apply IVT with hypotheses | Continuous f on [a,b], opposite signs **[key seed: root exists]** | Show x³+x−1 has a root in (0,1), then uniqueness by monotonicity **[target: f(x) = x³ + x − 1 is continuous on [0 ; 1], f(0) = −1 < 0 < 1 = f(1): a root exists (IVT); f′(x) = 3x² + 1 > 0, f strictly increasing: the root is unique]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-01-E2` confuse existence with uniqueness → "exactly one root" (`RP-12`, `RP-13`); `M1-LC-01-E4` *(v6)* differentiability required → "a root only if f is differentiable"; `M1-LC-01-E5` *(v8)* root placed at the midpoint → "a root at (a + b)/2" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-LC-01-E1` apply IVT without continuity → IVT used for a function not shown continuous (`RP-04`); `M1-LC-01-E3` omit endpoint signs → f(0) and f(1) not computed |
| **`M1-LC-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.06.7` | use Rolle or mean value theorem | If f(a)=f(b) and differentiable, ∃c with f'(c)=0 **[key seed: Rolle]** | Prove \|sin b−sin a\|≤\|b−a\| using MVT on suitable interval **[target: sin is differentiable and \|cos\| ≤ 1; MVT on [a ; b]: sin b − sin a = cos(c)(b − a), so \|sin b − sin a\| ≤ \|b − a\|]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-02-E4` *(v6)* theorems confused → "the intermediate value theorem"; `M1-LC-02-E5` *(v6)* conclusion too strong → "f′ = 0 everywhere" (`RP-12`); `M1-LC-02-E6` *(v8)* theorem confused → "Taylor's formula" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-LC-02-E1` forget differentiability interval → MVT applied without stating continuity on [a ; b] and differentiability on ]a ; b[ (`RP-04`); `M1-LC-02-E2` use endpoint derivative → cos a used for cos c; `M1-LC-02-E3` inequality reversed → \|sin b − sin a\| ≥ \|b − a\| |
| **`M1-LC-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.06.4` | establish continuity, differentiability and convexity | Differentiable at a implies **[key seed: continuous at a]** | Study convexity of x² and tangent supporting property via derivative **[target: f(x) = x²: f″ = 2 > 0 on ℝ, so f is convex; tangent at a: y = 2ax − a²; x² − (2ax − a²) = (x − a)² ≥ 0: the curve lies above its tangents]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-03-E1` assume converse → "continuity and differentiability are equivalent" (`RP-02`); `M1-LC-03-E4` *(v6)* continuity of the derivative claimed → "f′ is continuous at a"; `M1-LC-03-E5` *(v8)* differentiability taken to give monotonicity → "f is monotone near a" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-LC-03-E2` confuse second derivative sign → "f″ > 0, so f is concave"; `M1-LC-03-E3` omit interval → convexity stated without an interval (`RP-04`) |
| **`M1-LC-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MP2I.06.3` | use extreme value theorem on compact intervals | Continuous on [0,1] achieves max **[key seed: Yes: f is continuous on a closed bounded interval (segment)]** | State hypotheses for Weierstrass and give counterexample on open interval **[target: f continuous on a segment [a ; b] is bounded and reaches its bounds; on ]0 ; 1[, f(x) = x has no maximum (sup 1 not reached) and 1/x is not bounded]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-04-E1` replace closed interval by open → "Yes: f is bounded on ]0 ; 1[" (`RP-04`); `M1-LC-04-E2` assume mere boundedness implies attained → "Yes: f is bounded, so it reaches its bounds" (`RP-12`); `M1-LC-04-E3` ignore continuity → "Yes: every function on [0 ; 1] reaches its maximum" (`RP-04`) |
| **`M1-LC-05` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MP2I.06.2` | extend a function by continuity | Value at 0 that extends x ↦ sin(x)/x by continuity **[key seed: 1]** | Can x ↦ x ln x be extended by continuity at 0⁺? Value **[target: 0]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-05-E1` value of sin 0 given → 0; `M1-LC-05-E2` 0/0 taken to forbid the extension → "not extendable" (`CB-LIM-01`); `M1-LC-05-E3` 1/x factor taken as dominant → "+∞" |
| **`M1-LC-06` · D2** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.06.7` | apply the mean value inequality | \|f′\| ≤ 3 on [0 ; 2] and f(0) = 1. Best upper bound for f(2) from the mean value inequality? **[key seed: 7]** | Show that ln is 1-Lipschitz on [1 ; +∞[ **[target: \|ln′(x)\| = 1/x ≤ 1 there]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-06-E1` f(0) forgotten → 6; `M1-LC-06-E2` length of the interval forgotten → 4; `M1-LC-06-E3` length squared → 12 |

(f⁻¹)′(2)? **[key seed: 1/4]** | (arcsin)′(1/2) **[target: 2/√3]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-07-E1` f′ evaluated at 2 instead of f⁻¹(2) → 1/13; `M1-LC-07-E2` f′(1) not inverted → 4; `M1-LC-07-E3` 1/f(1) computed → 1/2 |
| **`M1-LC-08` · D2** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MP2I.06.9` | use concavity to compare a mean of values with the value at the mean | For x, y > 0, compare ln((x + y)/2) and (ln x + ln y)/2 **[key seed: "ln((x + y)/2) ≥ (ln x + ln y)/2, because ln is concave"]** | Deduce √(xy) ≤ (x + y)/2 **[target: apply exp, which is increasing]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-LC-08-E1` monotonicity used instead of concavity → "ln((x + y)/2) ≤ (ln x + ln y)/2, because ln is increasing"; `M1-LC-08-E2` ln treated as linear → "they are equal, by the properties of ln" (`CB-PUI-08`); `M1-LC-08-E3` right inequality, invalid reason → "ln((x + y)/2) ≥ (ln x + ln y)/2, because ln is increasing" |
