### `M1-AA` — Asymptotic analysis

- **Original coverage:** o, O, ~; Taylor-Young, limited expansions; asymptotics of sequences; Stirling (admitted).
- **Original expected capacities:** Compute expansions and equivalents.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Proof of Stirling not required.
- **Original source example:** « DL₂(0) de eˣ. » → 1 + x + x²/2 + o(x²)
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-LC
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M1-AA-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MP2I.11.1` | identify o, O and asymptotic equivalence | sin x ~ x as x→0 **[key seed: True: sin x/x → 1 as x → 0]** | Prove that ln(1 + x) ~ x as x → 0 **[target: ln(1 + x)/x → 1 (derivative of x ↦ ln(1 + x) at 0, or ln(1 + x) = x + o(x))]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-AA-01-E1` conflate O and o → "True, because sin x = O(x)" (O does not give ~); `M1-AA-01-E3` forget limiting regime → "True as x → +∞"; `M1-AA-01-E4` *(v8)* f ~ g taken as f − g → 0 → "True, because sin x − x → 0" <br>**Open seed:** `M1-AA-01-E2` change equivalence under subtraction → "sin x − x ~ x − x = 0" (`CB-LIM-03`) |
| **`M1-AA-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.11.2` | calculate Taylor expansions *(v7: `M1-AA-02-E1` has the value of the key in another form; options are compared as written, not by value; v8: the stem states the required form; E1 then checks that the remainder is written, not a value)* | Expansion of e^x at 0 to order 2, in the form a + bx + cx² + o(x²) **[key seed: 1+x+x²/2+o(x²)]** | Obtain order-3 expansion of sin x and estimate small-angle error **[target: sin x = x − x³/6 + o(x³); for x = 0.1 the error of sin x ≈ x is about x³/6 ≈ 1.7 × 10⁻⁴]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-AA-02-E1` omit remainder → 1 + x + x²/2 **[same value as the key in another form: the stem asks for that form]**; `M1-AA-02-E2` mistake factorial → 1 + x + x² + o(x²); `M1-AA-02-E4` *(v6)* order too low → 1 + x + o(x) <br>**Open seed:** `M1-AA-02-E3` use wrong parity terms → sin x = 1 − x²/2 + o(x²) |

### `M1-AR` — Arithmetic in ℤ

- **Original coverage:** Divisibility, GCD, Bézout, primes, p-adic valuation, congruences.
- **Original expected capacities:** Solve arithmetic problems with congruences.
- **Original stated exclusions:** ℤ/nℤ rings are OUT of program (2nd year).
- **Original source example:** « PGCD(84, 36) ? » → 12
- **Suggested prerequisites:** TE-AR (if taken), M1-LO

| **`M1-AR-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.07.5` | manipulate congruences without quotient-ring theory | 7² mod 5 **[key seed: 4]** | Prove if a≡b (mod m), then a²≡b² (mod m) **[target: m \| a − b and a² − b² = (a − b)(a + b), so m \| a² − b²: a² ≡ b² (mod m)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-AR-02-E1` treat congruence as equality → 49 (not reduced); `M1-AR-02-E4` *(v6)* square forgotten → 2; `M1-AR-02-E5` *(v6)* square computed as × 2 → 14 (`CB-PUI-05`) <br>**Open seed:** `M1-AR-02-E2` divide by nonunit unconditionally → 2a ≡ 2b (mod 4) "so a ≡ b (mod 4)"; `M1-AR-02-E3` change modulus inadvertently → "a² ≡ b² (mod m²)" |
| **`M1-AR-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MP2I.07.4` | study prime factors and valuations | v₂(40) **[key seed: 3]** | Find v₃(72) and v₂(72) **[target: 2 and 3]** | **MCQ seed:** `M1-AR-03-E1` count all factors instead of powers → 4 (number of prime factors with repetition); `M1-AR-03-E2` confuse valuations for different primes → 1 (v₅); `M1-AR-03-E3` neglect factorization → 8 (2³ given instead of the exponent) |

### `M1-ST` — Usual algebraic structures

- **Original coverage:** Groups, morphisms, rings, fields.
- **Original expected capacities:** Check a structure; use morphisms.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Artificial laws to be avoided.
- **Original source example:** « Le noyau d'un morphisme de groupes est-il un sous-groupe ? » → Oui
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-LO, M1-AR
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** **Guardrail:** ℤ/nℤ as a **ring** is second-year MPI. A first-year group-morphism seed involving quotient notation must be rewritten into a purely first-year morphism; do not require ring theory of ℤ/nℤ.

## 11. CPGE MPI — Year 2 (`MPI`)

**Official source gateway:** [BO no.31 / 26 Aug 2021 — MPI](https://www.education.gouv.fr/bo/21/Hebdo31/ESRS2111756A.htm). **Session gate:** see §1.1.

### `M2-ST` — Usual algebraic structures

- **Original coverage:** Groups (order, cyclic groups), ideals of ℤ and K[X], ℤ/nℤ, Chinese remainder theorem, Euler's φ, algebras.
- **Original expected capacities:** Use ℤ/nℤ and ideals in proofs.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Proof of d'Alembert-Gauss out of program.
- **Original source example:** « φ(12) ? » → 4
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-ST, M1-AR, M1-PO
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M2-ST-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MPI.01.4` | use congruence classes and units in Z/nZ | In Z/7Z, inverse of 3 **[key seed: 5]** | Find the invertible elements of Z/8Z, give inverses, justify by gcd **[target: units of ℤ/8ℤ: 1, 3, 5, 7 (gcd with 8 equal to 1); each is its own inverse: 3 × 3 = 9 ≡ 1, 5 × 5 = 25 ≡ 1, 7 × 7 = 49 ≡ 1]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-ST-01-E4` *(v6)* rational inverse → 1/3; `M2-ST-01-E5` *(v6)* additive inverse → 4; `M2-ST-01-E6` *(v8)* sign error, 3 × 2 = 6 ≡ −1 → 2 <br>**Open seed:** `M2-ST-01-E1` confuse representatives/classes → classes 3 and 11 counted as different; `M2-ST-01-E2` cancel non-units → 2x ≡ 2 (mod 8) "so x ≡ 1"; `M2-ST-01-E3` assume every nonzero class invertible → 1, 2, …, 7 all invertible in ℤ/8ℤ |
| **`M2-ST-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.01.4` | apply Chinese remainder theorem in coprime moduli | x≡1(mod 3), x≡2(mod 5) gives x≡ **[key seed: 7(mod 15)]** | Solve x≡2(mod 3), x≡3(mod 5) with full residue class **[target: x = 8 satisfies both (8 = 2·3 + 2 = 5 + 3); x ≡ 8 (mod 15)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-ST-02-E2` add residues directly → 3 (mod 15); `M2-ST-02-E3` incorrect modulus product → 7 (mod 8); `M2-ST-02-E4` *(v6)* one residue kept → 2 (mod 15) <br>**Open seed:** `M2-ST-02-E1` forget coprimality for uniqueness → uniqueness modulo 15 claimed without coprimality (`RP-04`) |
| **`M2-ST-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.01.4` | use Euler totient and cyclic-group order | phi(12) **[key seed: 4]** | Compute phi(15) and verify Euler congruence for a coprime to 15 **[target: φ(15) = φ(3)φ(5) = 8; a = 2: 2⁸ = 256 = 17 × 15 + 1 ≡ 1 (mod 15)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-ST-03-E1` count non-units as units → 11; `M2-ST-03-E4` *(v6)* multiplicativity used on non-coprime factors → 2 (φ(2)φ(6)) (`RP-04`); `M2-ST-03-E5` *(v6)* number of divisors → 6 <br>**Open seed:** `M2-ST-03-E2` use Fermat for composite modulus → a¹⁴ ≡ 1 (mod 15); `M2-ST-03-E3` confuse group order and element order → order of 2 modulo 15 (4) taken for φ(15) |
| **`M2-ST-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `MPI.01.3` | recognise ideals of Z and K[X] | Ideal generated by 6 and 10 in Z **[key seed: 2Z]** | Prove every ideal of Z is nZ, identifying the least positive generator **[target: I ≠ {0}: n = min(I ∩ ℕ*); for a ∈ I, a = qn + r with 0 ≤ r < n and r = a − qn ∈ I, so r = 0 and I = nℤ; I = {0} = 0ℤ]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-ST-04-E2` choose lcm not gcd → 30ℤ; `M2-ST-04-E4` *(v6)* product → 60ℤ; `M2-ST-04-E5` *(v8)* generators added → 16ℤ <br>**Open seed:** `M2-ST-04-E1` confuse ideal/subgroup closure → closure under multiplication by every integer not checked; `M2-ST-04-E3` neglect zero ideal → zero ideal forgotten |
| **`M2-ST-05` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MPI.01.1` | compute the order of an element in a cyclic group | Order of 4 in (ℤ/10ℤ, +) **[key seed: 5]** | Generators of (ℤ/10ℤ, +) **[target: {1 ; 3 ; 7 ; 9}]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-ST-05-E1` gcd(4 ; 10) given → 2; `M2-ST-05-E2` order of the group given → 10; `M2-ST-05-E3` opposite of 4 given → 6 |

### `M2-RED` — Reduction

- **Original coverage:** Stable subspaces, eigen-elements, characteristic and minimal polynomials, diagonalisation, trigonalisation, nilpotence, kernel lemma, Cayley-Hamilton.
- **Original expected capacities:** Decide diagonalisability; use annihilating polynomials.
- **Original stated exclusions:** « Valeur spectrale » out of program; trigonalisation practice not an objective.
- **Original source example:** « A nilpotente ⇒ Sp(A) ? » → ⊂ {0}
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-AL, M1-PO
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M2-RED-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MPI.02.3` | find characteristic polynomial and eigenvalues | Diagonal diag(2,3) has characteristic polynomial **[key seed: (X−2)(X−3)]** | Characteristic polynomial and eigenvalues of [[2, 1], [0, 3]] **[target: χ(X) = (X − 2)(X − 3) (triangular matrix); eigenvalues 2 and 3]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-RED-01-E1` use det(A+XI) → (X + 2)(X + 3); `M2-RED-01-E3` confuse eigenvectors with eigenvalues → (1 ; 0) and (0 ; 1); `M2-RED-01-E4` *(v6)* trace term forgotten → X² + 6 <br>**Open seed:** `M2-RED-01-E2` sign of characteristic polynomial → det(A − XI) in dimension 3 gives −χ (no effect on 2 × 2 matrices) |
| **`M2-RED-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.02.4` | distinguish diagonalisation criteria and eigenspace dimensions | A with 2 distinct eigenvalues in M2(C) **[key seed: diagonalizable]** | Determine whether [[1,1],[0,1]] is diagonalizable by computing eigenspaces **[target: χ = (X − 1)²; E₁ = Ker(A − I) = Ker [[0, 1], [0, 0]] = ℝ(1 ; 0), of dimension 1 < 2: not diagonalisable]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-RED-02-E4` *(v6)* symmetric case only → "diagonalisable only if A is symmetric"; `M2-RED-02-E5` *(v8)* diagonalisable confused with invertible → "invertible"; `M2-RED-02-E6` *(v8)* conclusion weakened → "trigonalisable only" <br>**Open seed:** `M2-RED-02-E1` assume characteristic roots suffice → "χ is split, so A is diagonalisable" (`RP-12`); `M2-RED-02-E2` wrong kernel → E₁ = ℝ²; `M2-RED-02-E3` confuse trigonalisation with diagonalisation → "triangular, so diagonalisable" |
| **`M2-RED-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.02.9` | apply annihilating and minimal polynomials | If A²=0 then eigenvalues belong to **[key seed: {0}]** | Use A²−3A+2I=0 to restrict spectrum to {1,2} and discuss diagonalizability **[target: P = X² − 3X + 2 = (X − 1)(X − 2) annihilates A, so Sp(A) ⊂ {1 ; 2}; P is split with simple roots, so A is diagonalisable (A = I, A = 2I and mixed cases are all possible)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-RED-03-E4` *(v6)* nilpotent read as "no eigenvalue" → ∅; `M2-RED-03-E5` *(v8)* identity case added → "{0 ; 1}"; `M2-RED-03-E6` *(v8)* A² = 0 confused with A² = I → "{−1 ; 1}" <br>**Open seed:** `M2-RED-03-E1` assume every annihilator minimal → "the minimal polynomial is X² − 3X + 2" (false for A = I); `M2-RED-03-E2` reverse spectral implication → "Sp(A) = {1 ; 2}" (`RP-12`); `M2-RED-03-E3` ignore square-free criterion → diagonalisability not deduced from the simple roots |
| **`M2-RED-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `MPI.02.6` | analyse nilpotent matrices and kernel lemma | Nilpotent matrix has determinant **[key seed: 0 (n≥1)]** | Prove a nilpotent endomorphism has no nonzero eigenvalue **[target: Aᵖ = 0; if Ax = λx with x ≠ 0, then 0 = Aᵖx = λᵖx, so λ = 0]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-RED-04-E2` conflate nilpotent and invertible → "invertible"; `M2-RED-04-E4` *(v6)* unipotent confused → 1; `M2-RED-04-E5` *(v8)* determinant taken as the dimension → n <br>**Open seed:** `M2-RED-04-E1` nilpotent means zero → "A nilpotent, so A = 0"; `M2-RED-04-E3` overclaim diagonalizability → "nilpotent, so diagonalisable" (`RP-12`) |
| **`M2-RED-05` · D1** *(v7)* (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MPI.02.2`, `MPI.02.3` | compute the eigenvalues of a 2 × 2 matrix | Eigenvalues of [[2, 1], [1, 2]] **[key seed: {1 ; 3}]** | An eigenvector for each eigenvalue **[target: (1, −1) for 1 and (1, 1) for 3]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-RED-05-E1` diagonal entries taken as eigenvalues → {2}; `M2-RED-05-E2` sign of det in χ wrong (X² − 4X − 3) → {2 − √7 ; 2 + √7}; `M2-RED-05-E3` trace and determinant given → {4 ; 3} |

### `M2-EU` — Endomorphisms of a Euclidean space

- **Original coverage:** Adjoint, orthogonal matrices, isometries, spectral theorem, positive self-adjoint endomorphisms.
- **Original expected capacities:** Diagonalise a real symmetric matrix in an orthonormal basis.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Hermitian products out of program.
- **Original source example:** « Une matrice symétrique réelle est-elle diagonalisable ? » → Oui (base orthonormée)
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-EU, M2-RED
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `M2-EVN` — Topology of normed spaces

- **Original coverage:** Norms, sequences, equivalent norms, open/closed sets, continuity, operator norm, compactness, path-connectedness, finite dimension.
- **Original expected capacities:** Prove continuity; use compactness for extrema.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Metric/topological spaces, Cauchy sequences, Banach, Borel-Lebesgue out of program.
- **Original source example:** « En dimension finie, les normes sont-elles équivalentes ? » → Oui
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-SU, M1-AL
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

### `M2-SEN` — Power series

- **Original coverage:** Radius, Abel's lemma, continuity and regularity of the sum, usual expansions.
- **Original expected capacities:** Compute radii; expand usual functions.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Proof of radial Abel theorem out of program.
- **Original source example:** « Rayon de Σ xⁿ/n ? » → 1
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M2-SF, M1-SN
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M2-SEN-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MPI.06.7` | find radius of convergence using ratio test on coefficients | sum x^n/n has radius **[key seed: 1]** | Radius of Σ n xⁿ and behaviour at the endpoints **[target: R = 1 ((n + 1)/n → 1); at x = ±1, n xⁿ does not tend to 0: divergence at both endpoints]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-SEN-01-E4` *(v6)* coefficients tending to 0 read as infinite radius → +∞; `M2-SEN-01-E5` *(v8)* limit of the coefficients taken as the radius → 0; `M2-SEN-01-E6` *(v8)* radius taken to depend on n → "it depends on n" <br>**Open seed:** `M2-SEN-01-E1` confuse term series with power series → radius of Σ nxⁿ taken from the limit of the terms; `M2-SEN-01-E2` include endpoint automatically → "converges on [−1 ; 1]"; `M2-SEN-01-E3` misuse radius inequality → \|x\| < R written as x < R |
| **`M2-SEN-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.06.9` | expand familiar functions into power series | sum x^n for \|x\|<1 **[key seed: 1/(1−x)]** | Differentiate geometric series within \|x\|<1 to derive sum n x^(n−1) **[target: for \|x\| < 1: Σ_{n≥1} n xⁿ⁻¹ = (1/(1 − x))′ = 1/(1 − x)² (term-by-term differentiation inside the disc, radius 1)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-SEN-02-E4` *(v6)* sign error → 1/(1 + x) (`CB-SGN-03`); `M2-SEN-02-E5` *(v6)* index shift → x/(1 − x) (`CB-NOT-05`); `M2-SEN-02-E6` *(v8)* derivative series used → 1/(1 − x)² <br>**Open seed:** `M2-SEN-02-E1` differentiate on boundary → result used at x = 1; `M2-SEN-02-E2` omit derivative coefficient → Σ xⁿ⁻¹ = 1/(1 − x)² (factor n forgotten); `M2-SEN-02-E3` wrong radius → radius 1/2 after differentiation |

### `M2-IN` — Integration on any interval

- **Original coverage:** Integrability, comparison, IPP and change of variable, dominated convergence, term-by-term integration, parameter integrals.
- **Original expected capacities:** Justify integrability and apply dominated convergence.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Semi-convergent integrals not an objective; proofs of dominated convergence out of program.
- **Original source example:** « ∫₀^{+∞} e^(−t) dt ? » → 1
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-IN, M2-SE
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

| **`M2-IN-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · `REASONED` · ref `MPI.08.1` | use comparison for improper integrals | For p>1, integral_1^infty t^(−p) converges **[key seed: True: ∫₁ˣ t⁻ᵖ dt = (1 − x^(1−p))/(p − 1) → 1/(p − 1)]** | Classify integral_1^infty 1/sqrt(t) dt using p-integral test **[target: 1/√t = t^(−1/2), p = 1/2 ≤ 1: ∫₁ˣ = 2√x − 2 → +∞: divergent]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-IN-02-E1` reverse p criterion → "False: it converges for p < 1"; `M2-IN-02-E3` conflate near zero/infinity → "True: it converges near 0 for p > 1"; `M2-IN-02-E4` *(v8)* integrand tending to 0 taken as sufficient → "True, because t^(−p) → 0 as t → +∞" <br>**Open seed:** `M2-IN-02-E2` ignore positivity → comparison used without positivity (`RP-04`) |
| **`M2-IN-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.08.5` | apply dominated convergence with stated bound | If \|f_n\|≤g integrable and f_n→0 pointwise, integral f_n **[key seed: 0 (under hypotheses)]** | For f_n(t)=e^(−nt) on [0,1] apply dominated convergence, determine integral limit **[target: fₙ(t) = e^(−nt) → 0 on ]0 ; 1] (1 at t = 0, a single point); \|fₙ\| ≤ 1, integrable on [0 ; 1]: ∫₀¹ fₙ → 0; directly (1 − e⁻ⁿ)/n → 0]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-IN-03-E4` *(v6)* one → 1; `M2-IN-03-E5` *(v6)* uniform convergence required → "nothing can be said without uniform convergence"; `M2-IN-03-E6` *(v8)* integral of the dominating function given → "∫ g" <br>**Open seed:** `M2-IN-03-E1` omit uniform domination → no dominating function given (`RP-04`); `M2-IN-03-E2` assume pointwise convergence at every endpoint → "fₙ → 0 on [0 ; 1]" (t = 0 ignored); `M2-IN-03-E3` confuse theorem statement with proof requirement → proof of the theorem attempted |

### `M2-PR` — Discrete random variables

- **Original coverage:** Countable sets, probability spaces, conditioning, usual laws (geometric, Poisson), expectation, variance, covariance, Markov, Bienaymé-Tchebychev, weak LLN, generating functions.
- **Original expected capacities:** Model with discrete laws; use generating functions.
- **Original stated exclusions:** σ-algebra manipulation, densities, conditional expectation, a.s./in-law convergence out of program.
- **Original source example:** « G_X(t) pour X ~ P(λ) ? » → e^(λ(t−1))
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-PR, M2-SE
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.

|---|---|---|---|---|

| **`M2-PR-04` · D3** (MCQ/Open 3 / 12 min) · ref `MPI.09.9` | apply concentration bound for discrete variables | If Var(X)=4, P(\|X−E(X)\|≥4)≤ **[key seed: 1/4]** | Use Chebyshev to bound P(\|sample mean−mu\|≥epsilon) in an iid finite-variance model **[target: V(X̄ₙ) = σ²/n; P(\|X̄ₙ − μ\| ≥ ε) ≤ σ²/(nε²) → 0 (weak law of large numbers)]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-PR-04-E3` interpret upper bound as exact value → "exactly 1/4"; `M2-PR-04-E4` *(v6)* σ²/a → 1; `M2-PR-04-E5` *(v6)* σ/a → 1/2 <br>**Open seed:** `M2-PR-04-E1` use Markov on signed variable → Markov's inequality applied to X − E(X) (any sign) (`RP-04`); `M2-PR-04-E2` forget n variance factor → σ²/ε² (n forgotten) (`CB-PRB-09`) |

For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

### `M2-ED` — Linear differential equations

- **Original coverage:** Cauchy linear theorem, solution structure, matrix exponential, constant-coefficient systems, variation of constants.
- **Original expected capacities:** Solve linear systems via reduction and exp(A).
- **Original stated exclusions:** Explicit solving of constant-coefficient systems not an objective.
- **Original source example:** « Solution de X′ = AX, X(0) = X₀ ? » → X(t) = e^(tA)X₀
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-TA, M2-RED
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** Matrix exponentials and structural methods are within the MPI programme; avoid turning explicit resolution of arbitrary constant-matrix systems into a compulsory high-volume objective.

### `M2-CD` — Differential calculus & optimisation

- **Original coverage:** Directional/partial derivatives, differential, gradient, chain rule, C¹/Cᵏ, tangent vectors, critical points, constrained extrema, Hessian.
- **Original expected capacities:** Find and classify extrema.
- **Original stated exclusions:** Implicit function theorem and second differential out of program.
- **Original source example:** « Points critiques de f(x, y) = x² + y² − 2x ? » → (1 ; 0)
- **Suggested prerequisites:** M1-F2 (if verified), M2-EVN, M2-EU
- **Eligibility:** `SOURCE-MAPPED · CHECK SESSION`. Subskills are design candidates, not officially certified line-by-line.
- **Correction / exception:** Hessian-based second-order optimisation is in MPI; the *second differential* itself and the implicit function theorem are excluded. Do not mistake these exclusions for a Hessian prohibition.

| Micro-skill / difficulty | Precisely observable learner action | MCQ seed and correct answer | Open-question seed and target | Proposed error patterns / distractor IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`M2-CD-01` · D1** (MCQ/Open 1 / 4 min) · ref `MPI.11.2` | compute partial derivatives and gradient | For f(x,y)=x²+y²−2x, grad f **[key seed: (2x−2,2y)]** | Find critical point of x²+y²−2x and evaluate function there **[target: ∇f = 0 ⇔ x = 1 and y = 0: critical point (1 ; 0), f(1 ; 0) = −1]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-CD-01-E1` differentiate wrong coordinate → (2y ; 2x − 2); `M2-CD-01-E4` *(v6)* −2x term forgotten → (2x ; 2y); `M2-CD-01-E5` *(v8)* partial derivative in y forgotten → (2x − 2 ; 0) <br>**Open seed:** `M2-CD-01-E2` only solve one equation → x = 1, y left free; `M2-CD-01-E3` confuse point and value → −1 given as the critical point |
| **`M2-CD-02` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.11.3` | use chain rule in several variables | For f(x,y)=x²+y², path (t,t²), derivative at t=1 **[key seed: 6]** | Compute derivative of f(cos t,sin t) with multivariable chain rule; state vector form **[target: g(t) = f(cos t ; sin t); g′(t) = ∂ₓf·(−sin t) + ∂ᵧf·cos t = 0 (g = 1); vector form g′(t) = ⟨∇f(γ(t)), γ′(t)⟩]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-CD-02-E1` forget inner derivative → 4 (∂ₓf + ∂ᵧf at (1 ; 1), γ′ forgotten); `M2-CD-02-E2` confuse partial and directional → 2 (∂ₓf only); `M2-CD-02-E3` evaluate at wrong point → 0 (evaluated at t = 0) |
| **`M2-CD-03` · D2** (MCQ/Open 2 / 8 min) · ref `MPI.11.8` | apply differentiability/Taylor formula to optimise | For f=x²+y² at 0, Hessian **[key seed: 2I]** | Use positive-definite Hessian at a critical point to conclude strict local min; explain hypotheses **[target: f of class C² on an open set, ∇f(a) = 0 and H(a) positive definite (eigenvalues > 0) ⇒ strict local minimum; e.g. x² + y² at (0 ; 0): H = 2I]** | **MCQ seed:** `M2-CD-03-E4` *(v6)* factor 2 forgotten → I; `M2-CD-03-E5` *(v8)* zero gradient read as a zero Hessian → 0; `M2-CD-03-E6` *(v8)* gradient given → "(2x ; 2y)" <br>**Open seed:** `M2-CD-03-E1` infer min from stationary alone → "∇f(a) = 0, so minimum" (`RP-12`); `M2-CD-03-E2` confuse determinant condition → "det H > 0, so minimum" (sign of the trace forgotten); `M2-CD-03-E3` attempt proof of second differential → second differential used (outside the programme) |

For proof questions reweight in favour of quantified logic and justification. **v6:** write each criterion with its expected intermediate result and the error code that explains its loss (§2C.5); take the codes from the row errors above. **Remediation:** error ID → targeted lesson → guided exercise → transfer exercise → equivalent re-test.

## 12. Fully specified student/teacher item pairs

These are **examples** of diagnostic items, not statements about the official exam format. Rewrite student texts into `{OUTPUT_LANGUAGE}` (French by default) and re-validate every key and signature on the final numbers. **v6:** every wrong option now has its distractor map, and four examples show the v6 formats (two-tier, find-the-error, staged open question, parametric item).

### `BREVET` — example pair (`BR-NC-04`)

**Student — MCQ:** Factor 9x² − 25.

A. (3x − 5)(3x + 5)  B. (x − 5)(x + 5)  C. (3x − 5)²  D. (3x + 5)²

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **A**. Distractor map: B → `BR-NC-04-E1` (`CB-PAR-04`, square of 3 forgotten); C → `BR-NC-04-E4` (`CB-LIT-03`, a² − b² taken as (a − b)²); D → `BR-NC-04-E2` (`CB-LIT-03`).

**Student — open:** Solve 9x² − 25 = 0 using factorisation and justify your result.

**Teacher — expected:** (3x − 5)(3x + 5) = 0, so x = 5/3 or x = −5/3.

**4-point marking rubric:** factorisation (1) [`BR-NC-04-E1`, `BR-NC-04-E2`]; zero-product property stated (1) [`CB-EQ-05`]; both solutions (1) [`BR-NC-04-E3`, `CB-EQ-03`]; check by substitution (1) [`CB-VER-02`].

### `BAC_1RE_SPE` — example pair (`1S-AL-01`)

**Student — MCQ:** Solve x² − 5x + 6 = 0.

A. {−2 ; −3}  B. {2 ; 3}  C. {4 ; 6}  D. {−1 ; 6}

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **B**. Distractor map: A → `1S-AL-01-E2` (sign of −b); C → `1S-AL-01-E1` (2a forgotten); D → `1S-AL-01-E4` (Δ = b² + 4ac).

**Student — open:** Solve 2x² − 3x − 2 = 0. Show the discriminant and the two roots.

**Teacher — expected:** Δ = 25; roots 2 and −1/2.

**4-point marking rubric:** coefficients and Δ = b² − 4ac (1) [`1S-AL-01-E4`]; formula with 2a (1) [`1S-AL-01-E1`, `1S-AL-01-E2`]; both roots (1) [`CB-EQ-03`]; one root checked (1) [`CB-VER-02`].

### `BAC_1RE_COMMUN` — example pair (`1C-PR-01`)

**Student — MCQ:** In a group of 1 000 people, 160 are sick; 200 test positive, of whom 150 are sick. What is P(sick \| positive)?

A. 1/4  B. 3/4  C. 0.15  D. 15/16

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **B**. Distractor map: A → `1C-PR-01-E4` (complement); C → `1C-PR-01-E2` (`CB-PRB-04`, divided by the whole group); D → `1C-PR-01-E1` (`CB-PRB-01`, P(positive \| sick)).

**Student — open:** In 1 000 people, 100 are sick; 90 sick and 45 healthy people test positive. Calculate P(sick \| positive) and explain the denominator.

**Teacher — expected:** 90/(90 + 45) = 2/3; the denominator counts all positive tests (not P(+ \| sick) = 0.9).

**4-point marking rubric:** denominator = all positives, 135 (1) [`CB-PRB-04`]; numerator 90 (1) [`CB-PRB-02`]; 2/3 (1); interpretation, distinguished from P(+ \| sick) (1) [`CB-PRB-01`].

### `BAC_TLE_SPE` — example pair (`TS-AN-02`)

**Student — MCQ:** Find lim_(n→∞) (2n² + 1)/(n² − 3).

A. −1/3  B. 1  C. 2  D. +∞

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **C**. Distractor map: A → `TS-AN-02-E2` (`CB-LIM-02`, constant terms kept); B → `TS-AN-02-E1` (`CB-LIM-01`, "∞/∞ = 1"); D → `TS-AN-02-E4` (`CB-LIM-02`, numerator alone).

**Student — open:** Compute lim_(x→+∞) (3x² − x)/(x² + 1), showing the dominant-term argument.

**Teacher — expected:** 3, after dividing numerator and denominator by x².

**4-point marking rubric:** indeterminate form recognised (1) [`CB-LIM-01`]; factorisation by x² (1) [`CB-FRA-05`]; limits of 1/x and 1/x² (1); conclusion 3 (1) [`CB-LIM-02`].

### `BAC_TLE_EXPERTES` — example pair (`TE-CX-01`)

**Student — MCQ:** What is \|1 + i\|?

A. 1  B. √2  C. 2  D. π/4

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **B**. Distractor map: A → `TE-CX-01-E5` (real part); C → `TE-CX-01-E2` (`CB-PUI-04`, modulus squared); D → `TE-CX-01-E4` (argument given).

**Student — open:** Write 1 + i in exponential form and verify by conversion to Cartesian form.

**Teacher — expected:** √2 e^(iπ/4).

**4-point marking rubric:** modulus √2 (1) [`TE-CX-01-E2`]; argument π/4 with its quadrant (1) [`TE-CX-01-E1`]; exponential form, argument modulo 2π (1) [`TE-CX-01-E3`]; reconstruction check (1) [`CB-VER-02`].

### `BAC_TLE_COMPLEMENTAIRES` — example pair (`TC-SE-01`)

**Student — MCQ:** A €1,000 capital increases by 3 % per year. Its value after n years is:

A. 1000 + 30n  B. 1000 × 0.03ⁿ  C. 1000 × 1.03ⁿ  D. 1000 × 1.03ⁿ⁻¹

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **C**. Distractor map: A → `TC-SE-01-E1` (`CB-PCT-03`); B → `TC-SE-01-E4` (`CB-PCT-04`); D → `TC-SE-01-E2` (`CB-NOT-05`).

**Student — open:** Compare the amounts after 3 years for €1,000 growing by €30/year and by 3 %/year; explain the different models.

**Teacher — expected:** 1 090 € versus 1 000 × 1.03³ = 1 092.727 €.

**4-point marking rubric:** additive model (1) [`CB-PCT-03`]; multiplicative model (1) [`CB-PCT-04`]; values without early rounding (1) [`CB-NUM-04`]; interpretation: the percentage applies to a growing base (1).

### `MP2I` — example pair (`M1-AL-01`)

**Student — MCQ:** Let u be a linear map of ℝ³ with rank 2. What is dim Ker u?

A. 0  B. 1  C. 2  D. 3

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **B**. Distractor map: A → `M1-AL-01-E3` (square matrix taken as injective); C → `M1-AL-01-E2` (dim Ker = rank); D → `M1-AL-01-E4` (dimension of the space).

**Student — open:** State the rank–nullity theorem and compute dim Ker u when dim E = 5 and rank u = 3.

**Teacher — expected:** dim Ker u = 2.

**4-point marking rubric:** theorem with its hypothesis (E finite-dimensional) (1) [`RP-04`]; substitution 5 = 3 + dim Ker u (1) [`M1-AL-01-E1`]; result (1); clear writing (1) [`RP-08`].

### `MPI` — example pair (`M2-SF-01`, `REASONED`)

**Student — MCQ:** For fₙ(x) = xⁿ on [0 ; 1], does (fₙ) converge uniformly?

A. Yes, since every fₙ is continuous  B. Yes, since xⁿ converges for every x  C. No: the pointwise limit is discontinuous at 1  D. Yes, since xⁿ → 0 on [0 ; 1]

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **C**. Distractor map: A → `M2-SF-01-E2` (`RP-12`); B → `M2-SF-01-E1` (`RP-09`, pointwise taken as uniform); D → `M2-SF-01-E3` (x = 1 forgotten).

**Student — open:** For fₙ(x) = xⁿ on [0 ; 1], find the pointwise limit and prove that the convergence is not uniform.

**Teacher — expected:** f = 0 on [0 ; 1[, f(1) = 1: f is discontinuous, and a uniform limit of continuous functions is continuous.

**4-point marking rubric:** pointwise limit on [0 ; 1[ (1) [`M2-SF-01-E3`]; value at 1 (1); theorem used by contradiction (1) [`M2-SF-01-E2`, `RP-12`]; conclusion with quantifiers in order (1) [`M2-SF-01-E1`, `RP-09`].

### `BREVET` — demonstration pair (`BR-EG-08`) — v5

**Student — MCQ:** A, M, B are aligned in this order, and so are A, N, C. AM = 3, AB = 5, AN = 4, AC = 7. Are (MN) and (BC) parallel?

A. Yes, because 3/5 ≈ 0.6 and 4/7 ≈ 0.6  B. No, because 3/5 ≠ 4/7  C. Yes, by Thalès' theorem, because the points are aligned  D. It cannot be decided without measuring the angles

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **B** (contrapositive of Thalès: 21/35 ≠ 20/35). Distractor map: A → `BR-EG-08-E4` (`RP-07`, rounded equality); C → `BR-EG-08-E3` (`RP-02`); D → `BR-EG-08-E5` (`RP-01`, measurement as proof).

**Student — open:** A, M, B and A, N, C are aligned in the same order. AM = 2.4, AB = 6, AN = 3, AC = 7.5. Prove that (MN) and (BC) are parallel.

**Teacher — expected:** AM/AB = 2.4/6 = 0.4 and AN/AC = 3/7.5 = 0.4; the ratios are equal and the points are aligned in the same order, so (MN) ∥ (BC) by the converse of Thalès' theorem.

**4-point marking rubric:** alignment and order stated (1) [`BR-EG-08-E1`, `RP-04`]; corresponding ratios AM/AB and AN/AC (1) [`BR-EG-08-E2`]; exact equality, no rounding (1) [`RP-07`]; converse named and conclusion (1) [`BR-EG-08-E3`, `RP-02`].

### `BAC_TLE_SPE` — tolerance pair (`TS-AN-06`) — v5

**Student — MCQ:** uₙ = 2 + 1/n for n ≥ 1. From which rank are all the terms in ]1.99 ; 2.01[?

A. n ≥ 100  B. n ≥ 101  C. n ≤ 100  D. Only for n = 101

**Teacher — MCQ answer:** **B** (1/n < 0.01 ⇔ n > 100; u₁₀₀ = 2.01 is not in the open interval). Distractor map: A → `TS-AN-06-E2` (`CB-EQ-07`); C → `TS-AN-06-E1` (`CB-INQ-01`); D → `TS-AN-06-E3` (`RP-05`).

**Student — open:** uₙ = 3 + 2/n for n ≥ 1. Find an integer N such that, for every n ≥ N, uₙ ∈ ]3 − 10⁻³ ; 3 + 10⁻³[. Justify that the condition holds for every n ≥ N.

**Teacher — expected:** \|uₙ − 3\| = 2/n < 10⁻³ ⇔ n > 2000, so N = 2001; since 2/n decreases, n ≥ 2001 ⇒ 2/n ≤ 2/2001 < 10⁻³.

**4-point marking rubric:** inequality \|uₙ − 3\| < 10⁻³ set up (1) [`CB-INQ-02`]; solved in the right direction (1) [`TS-AN-06-E1`]; N = 2001 with the right strictness (1) [`TS-AN-06-E2`]; "for every n ≥ N" justified (1) [`TS-AN-06-E3`].

### `BAC_1RE_SPE` — two-tier item (`1S-PS-02`) — v6

**Student — tier 1:** A screening test: prevalence 10 %, sensitivity 80 %, false-positive rate 10 %. A person tests positive. The probability that this person is ill is about:

A. 0.08  B. 0.47  C. 0.8  D. 0.89

**Student — tier 2:** I chose this answer because… 1. P(ill ∩ +)/P(+), with P(+) = 0.08 + 0.09. 2. The test detects 80 % of ill people. 3. 0.8/(0.8 + 0.1). 4. 10 % of 80 %.

**Teacher:** key **B + 1** (8/17 ≈ 0.47). Tier 1 map: A → `1S-PS-02-E3` (`CB-PRB-02`); C → `1S-PS-02-E1` (`CB-PRB-01`); D → `1S-PS-02-E2` (`CB-PRB-04`). Tier 2 map: 2 → `CB-PRB-01`; 3 → `CB-PRB-04`; 4 → `CB-PRB-02`. Reading (§2C.6): B + 1 acquired; B + another reason fragile (code of the reason); C + 2, D + 3 or A + 4 = misconception (`CONC`); other pairs = guess.

### `BREVET` — find-the-error item (`BR-NC-05`, `CB-SGN-05`) — v6

**Student:** Lina solves 3x − 5 = 2x + 7 and writes: « 3x − 2x = 7 − 5, donc x = 2. » Find her error, correct it, and check your answer.

**Teacher — expected:** the first line moves −5 without changing its sign; 3x − 2x = 7 + 5, so x = 12; check 3 × 12 − 5 = 31 = 2 × 12 + 7.

**4-point marking rubric:** wrong line located (1) [`CB-VER-04`]; error named: a term changes sign when it changes side (1) [`CB-SGN-05`]; x = 12 (1) [`CB-SGN-05`]; check by substitution (1) [`CB-VER-02`].

### `BAC_1RE_SPE` — staged open question (`1S-AU-02`) — v6

**Student:** A price of 50 € rises by 20 %, then falls by 20 %. a) Compute the final price. b) Give the overall multiplier and the overall percentage change. c) Explain why the price does not come back to 50 €.

**Teacher — expected:** a) 50 × 1.2 × 0.8 = 48 €; b) 0.96, i.e. −4 %; c) the fall of 20 % is taken on 60 €, a larger base than 50 €.

**4-point marking rubric (errors carried forward from a to b):** a) 48 € (1) [`CB-PCT-01` if 50 €; `CB-PCT-05` if computed in euros]; b) multiplier 0.96 (1) [`CB-PCT-04`]; −4 % (1) [`1S-AU-02-E5` if −0.04 %]; c) base 60 € named (1) [`CB-PCT-02`]. **Typical errors to tick:** "the two changes cancel" (`CB-PCT-01`); 0.96 written as −0.04 % (`1S-AU-02-E5`). **Re-test:** +10 % then −10 % on 200 € [198 €, −1 %].

### `BREVET` — parametric re-test item (`BR-DF-04`, YAML `param`, draft v1.1) — v6

The row rules become feedback formulas; the validator draws 200 variants and warns when a rule gives the key (masking) or when two rules coincide.

```yaml
- id: antecedent-affine
  type: param               # draft v1.1 (handoff): check that your player supports it
  skill_id: BR-DF-04
  difficulty: D2
  minutes: 2
  params:
    a: "randint(2, 9)"
    b: "randint(-9, 9, not 0)"
    x: "randint(-6, 6, not 0, 1)"
    c: "a*x + b"
  constraints: ["c != x", "a*c + b != x"]
  prompt: "f(x) = {a}x {b:signed}. Quel est l'antécédent de {c} par f ?"
  answers: [{id: r, label: "x =", value: "x", type: fraction}]
  feedback:
    - {answer: r, when: "reply == a*c + b", code: BR-DF-04-E1, tags: [CB-VER-04], text: "Tu as calculé l'image de {c}, pas son antécédent."}
    - {answer: r, when: "reply == (c + b)/a", code: CB-SGN-05, text: "Le terme {b:signed} a changé de membre sans changer de signe."}
    - {answer: r, when: "reply == (c - b)*a", code: BR-DF-04-E2, tags: [CB-EQ-01], text: "Il fallait diviser par {a}, pas multiplier."}
    - {answer: r, when: "reply == c/a", code: BR-DF-04-E3, text: "Le terme constant a été oublié."}
```

## 13. Integration with the diagnostic prompt (v6: T1–T10)

| Prompt output | Use of this reference | Quality gate |
|---|---|---|
| **T3 skills map** | Expand each selected parent code into its `SUBDOMAIN-NN` rows; keep eligibility and edition tags, prerequisites and difficulty. | Every row eligible for the school year; `HOLD`/`BRIDGE` only when the profile allows it. |
| **T4 error bank** | For each selected row: error IDs, rules, signatures **recomputed on the item's numbers**, type and 0–2 transversal codes (§2A, §2C.1); **(v8)** each error with its `mode`, its signature read on its own seed (R1). | No invented error; "documented" only for DEPP-flagged codes; signatures recomputed. |
| **T5 blueprint** | Skills × formats × difficulty × time, plus 4–8 **target codes** each planted in ≥ 2 items (§2C.7). | Σ time ≤ 0.9 × duration; code-coverage table present. |
| **T6 MCQ keys** | Per option: value → code → `why`; S1–S7 checks (§2C.2); reasoned options for binary seeds; **(v8)** MCQ only from `MCQ+OPEN` rows. | One key; distinct after normalisation; no masking; key positions spread. |
| **T7 open solutions** | Staged parts with intermediate results; rubric criteria carrying codes; typical errors; re-test (§2C.5). | Equivalent methods credited; error carried forward. |
| **T8 gap grid** | Evidence rules (§2C.6) and evidence states `SUSPECTED` / `CONFIRMED` / `UNDETERMINED` (§2C.6a): confidence matrix, `NSP`, `RECURRENT` within the test, minimum evidence. | No status from a single item; "indicatif" below 2 items or 3 points. |
| **T9 coverage** | Skills and codes of the selected blueprint: planned vs tested, D1/D2/D3, time. | Short sitting = sample; excluded skills listed. |
| **T10 follow-up** | One probe per observed code (§2A, new numbers), entries for the self-check card (§2B.8), re-test date. | Probes use new numbers and the same code. |
| **Error log** | CSV §2B.5 (+ `variant_seed` once the draft v1.1 parametric items are implemented). | Pseudonymised IDs; persistence per §2B.4. |

### Reference gate (built into prompt v6, Process steps 3–8)

```text
QUESTION-GENERATION REFERENCE GATE (v8)
1. Load this reference and select the school-year edition (§1.1).
2. Filter by {LEVEL}, then by the requested parent {SUBJECT} code(s).
3. Reject unverified and out-of-programme rows (eligibility and official anchor per row, Appendix A); use prerequisites only with an explicit PREREQUISITE tag; a PROVISIONAL anchor (COPY/TOC) is flagged in T3 and blocks "validated" until checked in the BO.
4. Choose 4–8 target codes and the rows that carry them (§2C.7); plant each target code twice.
5. Choose discriminating numbers (§2C.3); recompute the key and every signature on them.
6. MCQ: only from rows with allowed_items = MCQ+OPEN; one key and three distractors that pass S1–S7 (§2C.2); binary seeds as REASONED options.
7. Open: staged parts, intermediate results, rubric criteria with codes, typical errors, re-test (§2C.5).
8. In exam simulation, use the official session format (MCQ may be prohibited) and drop NSP / confidence.
9. Tag every error with its row error ID and 0–2 CB/RP codes; give each observation an evidence state (§2C.6a); update the error log (§2B).
10. Read every signature on its own seed (R1): sig_mcq with the MCQ seed, sig_open with the open seed; record the mode.
```

### Detection thresholds and reporting note

- A single MCQ alone does **not** justify a reliable micro-skill mastery category. With one item, report **provisional evidence**. Prefer 2–3 independent observations or a short follow-up open item for targeted diagnoses.
- Preserve the prompt's four categories (≥ 80 %, 50–79 %, 30–49 %, < 30 %) *only* when sufficient points are available; record small-sample caveats.
- Avoid labelling the same student error as evidence of multiple independent deficits unless the item explicitly tests a prerequisite.
- For MPI, retain the constraint of at least 20 % MP2I-coded prerequisites plus ≥ 1 rigorous proof and ≥ 1 student-error critique, and include these in the coverage computation.
- A transversal code observed once is `OBSERVED`, not a diagnosis; `RECURRENT` needs ≥ 2 observations (§2B.4).
- **(v6)** A wrong answer counts as a misconception only under §2C.6 (sure answer, two surfaces, or the rule defended).
- **(v7)** Only `CONFIRMED` evidence supports a misconception; `UNDETERMINED` evidence never names one error (§2C.6a).

**Source boundary:** original `Content`, `Expected`, `Limits`, and `Example` lines are taken from the uploaded DOCX, **not** quoted directly from the BO. The detailed micro-skills, example data, answer seeds and distractor candidates are an authored pedagogical expansion. The cited official sources support **programme version selection and high-level scope**; they do not retroactively certify each expanded row. Rows explicitly marked `HOLD` must not enter a final student assessment without verification. **v5:** quotations in the Brevet and Terminale correction notes were taken from R12–R16 as consulted on 19 October 2026; DEPP rates come from R17 as reported there; transversal codes without a DEPP reference are design hypotheses. **v6:** signatures, completed targets and changed seeds are authored computations (numeric ones re-checked by script, §15); R20–R21 support the item formats only, not any error code or rate.

Coverage: 351/600 rows with a key check, 949/2177 active errors with a signature check, 825/1288 numeric signatures checked, 901 text signatures for review, 50 symbolic infinities (25 checked); v6 checks linked to the CSV 163; NOT_SUPPORTED rows 6; CODEX v5 301 IDs enumerated 301 (reviewed 0); items (paper) PASS 0, HOLD 594, FAIL 6. | Build | `build_v9.py` at the package root, inputs shipped in `build/`, `--check` rebuilds and compares hashes |
| Prompt | companion prompt v7 |

| Change | Detail |
|---|---|
| Reading by seed | errors cell split into **MCQ seed:** / **Open seed:**; CSV `mode` (`mcq`, `open`, `mcq+open`), `sig_mcq`, `sig_open`; rule R1 (§2C.1); the three withdrawn "mismatches" were open-seed signatures (§1.2 item 23) |
| MCQ distractors | 107 new errors on 94 rows (*(v8)*); `allowed_items` (`MCQ+OPEN` / `OPEN_ONLY`); `TC-IN-02` `OPEN_ONLY`; `TS-AN-14-E1` on the open seed only (item 24) |
| Merge | `BR-AP-01-E3` merged into `E1`, never reused (item 25) |
| Required form | stems of `BR-NC-02`, `BR-NC-03`, `M1-AA-02`, `M1-PO-03` state the form; `M2-ED-03` compared as written (`compare` = `text`; E1 is a different matrix) (item 26) |
| Anchors | `anchor_status`; 158 `PROVISIONAL` rows marked in their heads and listed in `Anchor_Verification_Worklist_v8.csv`; sources R32–R35 (item 27) |
| Migration | `Math_ID_Migration_v5_v6_v7_to_v8.csv`: relations `MERGED` and `NEW_V8` added; stem changes as `SEED_REVISED` |
| Validation | `validate_reference_v8.py`: every row re-rendered from the CSV; `allowed_items`, `mode` and `anchor_status` rules; masking and collision scans on each seed (the 12 errors with a trace on both seeds are now scanned on the open seed too); explicit scope; runs with `python -I`; reports a missing `sympy` instead of crashing (§15) |
| Prompt | companion prompt v6 (default reference v8, rule R1, `allowed_items`, provisional anchors, YAML eligibility mapping) |


### v6 → v7

| Change | Detail |
|---|---|
| Diagnostic targets | 331 new micro-skills (600 in total) across the 8 tracks, one per official capability not yet covered; new subdomain `TC-LO`; IDs continue each subdomain's numbering |
| Official referential | Appendix A indexes 355 official programme entries (paraphrased, with verification status) from R3, R4, R15 and R24–R31; 302 are anchors of at least one row; every row carries `ref` |
| Foundation skills | `BR-NC-06`…`29`: 24 basic-calculation rows with standard IDs (Codex's `BR-NC-F*` IDs reserved) |
| Errors | 993 new `E1`–`E3` with signatures computed on the seeds; `M1-F2-02-E4` retired (padding) |
| Collisions and masking | `TS-AN-04` seed, `1C-AU-04`, `M1-F2-02` and `M1-AA-03` as `REASONED`, `BR-DF-03` staged open question, `BR-AP-01` declared ambiguous, 5 form errors declared; all rows scanned |

| Corrections | §1.2 item 3 (derivatives are in the 1C 2026 scope), item 2 (`M1-F2` in the 2021 MP2I programme, per-row eligibility), items 17–22 (lineage, reserved IDs, anchors, unindexed 2027 texts) |

| Change | Detail |
|---|---|
| Diagnostic precision layer (§2C) | Distractor rules S1–S7, trap table for choosing numbers, 12 diagnostic item formats (reasoned options, two-tier, confidence tier, « Je ne sais pas », find-the-error, staged open questions, matched pairs, parametric items…), open-question rubric with codes, evidence rules, target-code planning, mapping to `alphavalon.eval` |
| Error signatures (§4–11) | Every error of every row now gives the wrong answer it produces on the seed; 272 errors `E4`–`E6` added where E1–E3 could not give three distinct MCQ distractors; transversal tags inline in the rows (411 tagged errors) |
| Completed targets | The 161 open targets "To be worked and independently verified" computed |
